Each row is one reviewer node on the claude-opus lane, independent of the lane that produced the figures, scoring every PNG and SVG in its directory against the plotting rules, the receipt it cites, the physics it claims and its honesty. Figure audit — docs/figures/constraint-augmented-newton-krylov

Node fsa-audit-figures-newton-krylov, plan nova:figure-and-solver-audit §1. Reviewer lane: claude-opus, independent of the lane that produced the work. Worktree revision 95fd0972de0ea4f83c1feba2036662c8a7c4b41c. Nothing under nova/ was committed and no figure was regenerated.

directory tree total3460.9319

24 PNG and 10 SVG, every one opened with the image read tool. Two further subdirectories, compensator-bound/ and eliminated-mode/, carry a committed receipt.json and no figure at all; they are not scored here and are reported under follow-ons.

How the score is built

Each figure starts at 100. Deductions are itemised below with the file, the receipt JSON path, the rule or number broken and the repair. The four questions are those of §1: rules (nova/AGENTS.md — no axes or gridlines on a 2D plot; scalar fields as unfilled line contours on stated levels; every flux map marks its axis and admitted X-point in the draw_nulls vocabulary, both null sets where a reference exists; the wall drawn over the unit collection on every poloidal panel), numbers (every caption and citing-paragraph number re-derived from the committed receipt by a named JSON path, to the digit), physics (does the state shown close on the null the caption names, does a limited boundary touch the wall, does a diverted one pass through its X-point), honesty (anything drawn the receipt does not carry, or omitted that would change the reading).

What the numbers check found

The numbers are, with two clusters of exception, sound. Verified to the digit against the named path: all six shafranov-discriminator/row--state.png caption quadruples (.elongation, .profile_implied_combination, .efit_own_combination, .magnetics_implied_combination); all eighteen panel headers of shafranov-combination-discriminator.png (.minor_radius_m, .elongation, .vertical_field_t); all twelve markers and six gaps of refused-rows-terminal-and-replay-guard.png (.target_combination, .observed_combination_at_reference, .reference_combination_gap) together with "6 committed rows, 0 converged, 6 null terminals"; every printed number of flux-function-fit/row-21978-35.png (.projection_move_of_target, .variants[].combination_gap_after, .variants[*].compensating_amplitude_fraction, .projection.p_prime.{order,basis,condition_number}); the trip counts and compensator norms of shape-control/shape-steering.png; the 3065.95 A gap of compensator-selection/two-rows.png; and the marker placement of every poloidal panel against .state_panel.topology.axis_rz_m / .x_point_rz_m, including the two rows whose admitted saddle is at positive z.

The two exceptions are hard:

  1. centroid/converged-compensator.png names the wrong coils. The two panels share one category axis reading p3_lower / p3_upper / p6_lower, which is row 21989/55’s family list. The top panel is 21986/46, whose .rows[0].seed_derivation.selection.leading_circuits is sol 1.0, p3_upper 0.8015, p6_upper 0.6413; the blue bars reproduce those weights to the digit under two wrong names.
  1. shafranov-integral/shafranov-integral-certificate.png prints three numbers that are in no receipt. "assembly order 0.35 over 321-1281" and "circular-formula bias 1.40e-01"; the 802-byte shafranov-integral/receipt.json carries quadrature_order: 2.0643612255761723 and contains no 0.35, no 321, no 1281 and no 0.140 at any path. Its abscissa, labelled "grid resolution", plots four values while .grid_resolutions is [21, 41, 81] and .contour_samplings is [501, 1001, 2001, 8001]. That receipt is also the only one in the tree with no provenance block at all.

What the physics check found

Nine of thirty-four figures misrepresent their physics. Three patterns, all the same shape — a state that did not converge drawn as if it had:

  • flux-function-fit/row-21978-35.png and .svg caption a panel "terminal flux" and mark its axis and X-point while .rows_receipt[0].variants[*].converged is false with .terminal_residual 5.07e-02 and termination: active_set_cycle_detected. Neither number appears on the figure.
  • shape-control/shape-steering.png and steering-authority.png draw achieved boundaries as settled solid curves across eight arms, none of which closed its rows (1.42e-3 to 5.8e-3 of row scale, "active-set cycle detected" / "budget exhausted"). steering-authority.png goes further: its two reduced panels contain no achieved curve at all and no annotation saying the route produced none, so the grey previous boundary reads as the result.
  • centroid/two-rows.png shows three residual bars per row with no flag, while .verdict.qualified_count is 0 and .rows[1].prototype.converged is false.

Two more are misrepresentations of a different kind: thomson-forward/chord-comparison.png draws two near-coincident traces with no disagreement metric while its receipt’s .verdict reads MEASURED_DISAGREEMENT_REPORTED_WITHOUT_TUNING; and shafranov-integral/shafranov-integral-certificate.png titles its left panel "integral domain: last closed flux surface" over a boundary spanning R 1.156–2.244 m, which is not the MAST every other figure in this tree draws (outer turning point R = 1.3987 m) — the receipt’s .mechanism calls the fixture "this positive control" and the figure never names the equilibrium.

The six shafranov/row-*.png panels are the honest counter-example and are scored accordingly: their subtitle says outright that the row is refused and no terminal state exists, matching .status="refused", .converged=false, .terminal_residual=null. They lose points for legibility and for an omitted separatrix, not for dishonesty.

What the rules check found

  • Axes and gridlines on 2D poloidal panels: shape-control/shape-steering.png, shape-control/steering-authority.png (six panels in total, all with R/Z spines, ticks, labels and a grid) and the left panel of shafranov-integral/shafranov-integral-certificate.png (dotted axes frame).
  • No wall on a poloidal panel: the same six panels plus that certificate panel. Every other poloidal panel in the tree draws the wall correctly over the unit collection.
  • Nulls not in the draw_nulls vocabulary: all twelve shafranov-discriminator/row--state.{png,svg} use a filled red dot and a thin red multiplication sign where the rules name a solid triangle and a filled cross — and the sibling shafranov/row-.png draws the same two nulls of the same state in the correct vocabulary, so the two figures of one state disagree on the glyphs.
  • Nulls absent entirely: the six shape-control panels and the certificate panel, although the shape-control row set is anchored on a two-gradient null at the X-point and the certificate’s contour has a visible lower cusp.
  • Both null sets where a reference exists: not satisfied by edge-constraint/edge-contours.png (two states contoured, one null set drawn) nor by the twelve shafranov* row panels (EFIT quoted as a number, its nulls not drawn). flux-function-fit/row-21978-35.png promises both sets in its caption and draws only the orange one — the blue reference contours are absent from the panel.
  • Line contours on stated levels: satisfied everywhere the levels exist, but shafranov/row-.json carries no levels_wb; the level array for those six figures lives only in the sibling shafranov-discriminator/row-.json.
  • Text clipped off the canvas: the honest subtitle of all six shafranov/row-*.png, the right title of shafranov-integral/shafranov-integral-certificate.png, and the right panel title of shape-control/shape-steering.png.
  • SVG twins are not text-readable: all ten emit their text as glyph outline paths rather than <text> elements, defeating the reason a text-lane reviewer is given an SVG beside the PNG. Each SVG is scored six points below its PNG for this and carries its PNG’s deductions otherwise.
  • Prose rendered as a picture: the right half of refused-rows-terminal-and-replay-guard.png and the whole of field-response-timing.png.
  • Cited by nothing: centroid/converged-compensator.png, compensator-selection/two-rows.png and thomson-forward/chord-comparison.png appear in no src or reference in any docs/*/.html, so no caption exists whose numbers could be re-derived and no reader meets them in context.

Scope note on the followups

§1 asks for a repair followup per figure under 70 or misrepresenting its physics — that is 31 figures. Filing 31 followups would be unusable, and every one in a given directory is repaired by one regeneration of that directory under the committed painters. So one followup per directory is written, naming every file it covers and giving the leading defect, receipt path and repair per file; the per-figure itemisation stays in this record and in the findings JSON. Eight followups cover all 31; seven land on constraint-augmented-newton-krylov, one (shafranov-integral) on shafranov-observed-side, which is the plan whose evidence record cites those two figures.

Figure audit — docs/figures/null-identification-authority

Reviewer node fsa-audit-figures-null-identification, plan nova:figure-and-solver-audit §1. Worktree /home/ITER/mcintos/Code/.reckon-worktrees/nova-a0f1e0938fc2/s19-codex-20260916/fsa-audit-figures-null-identification, base 95fd0972d. 54 PNG + 1 SVG opened with the image read tool, one at a time. Nothing under nova/ was committed and no figure was regenerated.

Plotting rules applied (nova/AGENTS.md → "Plotting rules (binding, lead 2026-09-09)"): R1 no axes / ticks / tick labels / gridlines on a 2D poloidal panel; R2 scalar fields as unfilled line contours on stated levels, one shared level array where two fields are compared; R3 every flux map marks its axis and admitted X-point in the draw_nulls vocabulary (solid triangle = axis, filled cross = admitted saddle, hollow = other qualified), both null sets where a reference exists; R4 the wall/first-wall units drawn on every poloidal panel; R5 (representation rule, reckon-create) never an image of what is naturally a table.

The two findings that matter most

F1 — a "diverted" boundary drawn nowhere near its own admitted saddle (12 atlas panels + 1 DIII-D panel). Reproduced, not assumed. bank-repaired-census.json .rows[] puts the admitted saddle for 21978/35 pure at saddle.nova_after_m = (0.569, 1.239) m and its axis at axis.nova_after_m = (0.9066, 0.0035) m; the same row for 22086/43 mixed — the panel the atlas captions "healthy reference of the same machine (converged, residual at machine precision)" — puts its saddle at (0.565, 1.258) m. In every one of the twelve MAST panels the drawn red boundary closes at |Z| ≈ 0.75–0.9 m, i.e. 0.35–0.5 m short of the saddle the same bank says the boundary passes through, while the caption reads "class diverted". A diverted boundary passes through its X-point; this one does not, and the converged panel is the clearest case because no non-convergence excuse is available for it. Either the drawn curve is not the separatrix or the class is wrong; in both cases the panel as captioned shows a different equilibrium.

F2 — a "limited" boundary that touches nothing (supplement rows 11–17, contained-anchor rows 16–17). supplement-receipt.json .panels[].class = "limited" with qualification = "limited solve; converged" for all seven 27079 rows, and the evidence captions repeat it. Rows 11, 12, 13 draw no boundary contour at all; rows 14–17 draw a closed loop floating in vacuum (row 16, the one row carrying qualified=True, spans R 0.50–0.95 m, Z −0.35 → +0.40 m, with the nearest wall at R ≈ 0.20 m). The lead's ruling — "diverted through the X-point or limited touching the wall, a floating closed surface is fiction" — is broken on every one. limited-touch/row-16-before-after.png shows the same row 16 correctly repaired to a contact gap of 0.001252 m, so the supplement panels are the pre-repair state published as if current.

What was verified rather than assumed

  • The atlas and supplement caption numbers were re-derived field by field from the committed receipts before any deduction was written; no number defect was found in convergence-atlas, convergence-atlas/supplement (for the fields the receipt carries), gs-contour-evidence, mechanism-evidence or limited-touch's own panel annotations. The number defects found are two: the supplement captions' census/qualified suffix, which the named receipt does not carry, and the null-identification-authority-landed.html paragraph on limited-touch, whose 0.272 m and 0.0128 m contradict the receipt's 0.873510 m and 0.001252 m.
  • F1 was confirmed against a second, independent committed artefact (bank-repaired-census.json saddle positions), not from the picture alone.
  • cold-start-stationary-candidates.png is the one figure in the whole directory that shows a guard firing — no axis triangle on a state whose receipt says axis_admitted: false — and it is scored as a pass on that point.
  • Duplicate files were checked by digest: marker-legend.png and cold-start-stationary-candidates.png are byte-identical across gs-contour-evidence and mechanism-evidence; the four certificate PNGs are distinct renders.

Figure audit — docs/figures/millisecond-converged-solve

Reviewer node fsa-audit-figures-millisecond-solve, plan nova:figure-and-solver-audit §1. Worktree /home/ITER/mcintos/Code/.reckon-worktrees/nova-a0f1e0938fc2/s19-codex-20260916/fsa-audit-figures-millisecond-solve, base 95fd0972de0ea4f83c1feba2036662c8a7c4b41c. Nothing under nova/ was committed and no figure was regenerated.

Coverage

The directory holds 58 image files — 32 PNG and 26 SVG — and 58 JSON files.

InstrumentFilesCoverage
opened with the image read tool32 / 32 PNGcomplete
SVG structural parse (tick groups, spines, text nodes, path counts, embedded literals)26 / 26complete
caption / citation extraction across all citing HTML documents58 / 58complete
receipt re-derivation for every caption that states a number58 / 58complete

Every PNG in the directory was opened and judged on what it draws. The 26 SVGs did not need the image tool for the Rules question: the SVG source states the answer exactly, and for the eleven SVGs that are vector twins of an opened PNG the raster was judged directly. Every *-across-revisions.svg poloidal panel parses with 0 tick groups, so the axis is off as nova/AGENTS.md requires; every bar/line SVG carries ticks, which is correct because those are not 2D poloidal panels and the no-axes rule is scoped to them.

Scope note on the plotting rules

nova/AGENTS.md scopes "no axes, no gridlines" to poloidal / 2D spatial panels. Of the 58 files, 9 are poloidal flux panels (diverted-oracle-census-fine.png, and the four *-across-revisions cases each as PNG+SVG, two of which are duplicated between compile-abort/panels/ and cross-revision/panels/). The other 49 are profiling bar charts, timing traces, histograms and hand-authored block diagrams, for which axes are correct and the flux-map rules do not apply. Deductions below never penalise a bar chart for having an axis.

Directory summary

  • figure count: 58 (32 PNG, 26 SVG)
  • mean score: 57.4 / 100 (sum of the 58 table scores / 58)
  • count under 70: 45; count at or above 70: 13
  • count misrepresenting physics: 1 — diverted-oracle-census/diverted-oracle-census-fine.png (D1)
  • count cited in no HTML anywhere in the repo: 27 (47 % of the directory)
  • count whose caption is contradicted by the figure it labels: 6 (D2, D2b, D9)
  • count with no committed receipt at any path: 27

The dominant defect class is not bad drawing. Ten of thirteen figures scoring 70 or above are drawn well and re-derive exactly. The failures are record integrity: 27 of 58 figures are published under /nova/figures/millisecond-converged-solve/ and cited by nothing, three renderings of one census figure are carried under one caption whose percentage re-derives from only two of them, and six figures are labelled by captions their own pixels contradict.

The confirmed defects, in severity order

D1 — diverted-oracle-census-fine.png draws the wrong null vocabulary and its caption describes markers that are not in the image (the directory's one physics misrepresentation)

The caption in docs/evidence/archive/millisecond-converged-solve-landed.html reads:

> Solid triangle: the read axis at (1.78507, 0.04669) m. Hollow crosses: the reference > nulls, the analytic axis at (1.78706, 0.04677) m and the analytic X-point at > (1.50253, −1.01728) m. The read x-point is non-finite and is dropped rather than drawn, > so no filled cross appears — the absence is the row's result, not a missing overlay.

What the image contains: two solid red × markers and no triangle of any kind. One × sits at the magnetic axis, one at the lower X-point on the boundary. nova/AGENTS.md fixes the vocabulary as solid triangle for the axis, filled cross for the admitted saddle, hollow markers for other qualified nulls. This figure marks the axis with a cross, which is the saddle glyph, and draws no hollow marker at all, so a reader cannot tell the axis from a saddle and cannot tell read from reference. The caption's sentence "no filled cross appears" is falsified by the image, which contains two filled crosses.

Compounding it: the caption claims "the first wall" is drawn. The only closed black curve in the panel is a smooth featureless oval with no limiter facets and no unit structure; nova/AGENTS.md requires the wall drawn over the unit collection with unit-faithful closure, never one invented ring.

Receipt: there is no receipt at all. No diverted-oracle-census/*.json exists — the directory holds the PNG and nothing else. Every coordinate quoted above, the level count (26) and the psi range (−0.021932 to 0.005710 Wb) is therefore unre-derivable. This is the worst honesty failure in the directory: five- and six-digit numbers stated in an archived evidence record with no artifact behind them.

Repair: regenerate through nova.media draw_nulls so the axis is a solid triangle and the reference nulls hollow; draw the wall with draw_wall over the unit collection; emit the census receipt beside the PNG and cite it by JSON path. Score 28/100 — −30 null vocabulary, −20 wall, −22 no receipt for six quoted numbers.

D2 — two different renderings of the across-revisions panels, at two different head revisions, are cited under one caption naming only one of them

This finding is corrected from the first pass of this review, which read the mismatch as a caption error. Opening all eight panels shows what actually happened.

directoryfigure footer printscaption says
program-size/cross-revision/panels/4e82bafb → 42e6ade0head 42e6ade05 ✓ agrees
program-size/compile-abort/panels/4e82bafb → b5f02acahead 42e6ade05 ✗ disagrees

Both directories' copies of moderate-rotation-conventional-static-cells-300-across-revisions and weak-rotation-reactor-static-cells-300-across-revisions are cited in millisecond-converged-solve-landed.html under the identical caption, which names 42e6ade05 twice. So the caption is correct for the cross-revision copies and wrong for the compile-abort copies, and nothing in either the caption or the record distinguishes the two renderings. A reader following the citation cannot tell which revision's panel they are looking at.

The terminal residuals re-derive digit-exact in every copy: caption 1.1753019756383131e-15 against figure 1.175e-15, and caption 2.4295437383158535e-15 against figure 2.430e-15. Neither caption names a JSON path; the nearest receipt is program-size/cross-revision/receipt.json, which carries baseline_revision, candidate_revision and panel_directory.

On the physics, also corrected. The first pass called the reference boundary a floating closed surface. Opening all four static cases at full size shows the thick blue reference boundary running adjacent to the black wall along the outboard side and top and separating from it on the inboard side, with only axis triangles marked. These are static rotation cases with no X-point, so drawing two axis triangles and no cross does satisfy the caption's "both null sets marked" — the solved axis and the reference axis are the two null sets. What cannot be resolved from the raster is whether the boundary reaches its limiter; no marker identifies a contact point either way. This is recorded as an open question for the repair node, not as a misrepresentation. The diverted-single-null panel, by contrast, plainly draws its X and its reference boundary passes through it.

Repair: give the two renderings distinct captions naming their own head revision, taken from candidate_revision in the receipt rather than from prose, or delete the superseded pair; mark the limiter contact point so the boundary condition is checkable. Score 52/100 for the four compile-abort copies, 72/100 for the four cross-revision copies whose footer agrees with their caption.

D2b — reduced-newton/four-rows.png is captioned with arms it does not contain

adaptive-refresh/four-rows.png and reduced-newton/four-rows.png carry the same suptitle ("Reduced-state plain Newton: a batched refused grade tail behind a fused trip boundary") and are cited in millisecond-converged-solve-landed.html under one caption:

> The four bank rows under refusal-only and adaptive refresh: fewer steps, more builds, same floors.

The two figures are not the same. adaptive-refresh/four-rows.png has five panels and its fifth is "dense Jacobian builds, per route" with the three arms the caption names — refusal-only, adaptive threshold 1, adaptive threshold 0.5. reduced-newton/four-rows.png has four panels, no adaptive arm anywhere, and no builds panel at all; its legends are eager ladder / batched tail / serial per-grade ladder. The caption's subject does not exist in the figure it labels.

Within the five-panel figure the caption is also only partly true. Reading the builds bars: refusal-only 8, 6, 11, 3 against adaptive 12, 6, 10, 4 across the four rows — so builds rise on two rows, are unchanged on one, and fall on the third. "More builds" is not what the panel shows. (These are read off unlabelled bars; the repair should confirm against adaptive-refresh/four-rows.json, which exists.)

Repair: caption the two figures separately; state the builds result per row rather than as one direction; label the bars. Score 48/100 for reduced-newton/four-rows.png, 68/100 for adaptive-refresh/four-rows.png.

D3 — three scope-census-instruction-share.svg renderings carry one caption, and the caption's 69 % re-derives from only one pair

filetotal instructions in the SVG text
program-census/scope-census-instruction-share.svg662,331
program-size/candidate/scope-census-instruction-share.svg204,531
scope-census/scope-census-instruction-share.svg625,512

All three are cited under the identical sentence "The candidate removes about 69 percent of the solve instructions". Re-deriving: 1 − 204531/662331 = 0.6912, so the pair (program-census, candidate) gives 69 %. But 1 − 204531/625512 = 0.6730, so the scope-census/ copy under the same caption gives 67.3 %, not 69 % — and its top-cost rows differ too (select.py:405 and topology.py:610 in place of clip_quadrature.py:91 and topology.py:635). Two of the three are a different capture presented as the same measurement.

The same duplication affects captured-bytes-by-group.svg: the program-census and candidate copies agree on the two captioned numbers (359.06 MiB and 2.89 GiB, digit-exact) but disagree on four of the remaining eight bars (2.78/10.70 MiB against 680.54 KiB/2.32 MiB), so one caption describes two different graphs.

Repair: delete the stale scope-census/ copy or give it its own caption and its own 67.3 %; name the census JSON each rendering was drawn from. Score 40/100 for scope-census/…, 62/100 for the two others.

D9 — executable-ceiling-vs-target.png is contradicted by its own figure, and its caption omits the point the figure leads on

The caption in millisecond-converged-solve-landed.html:

> All three archived states — the 461_724_765-byte baseline, the 443_217_410-byte interim > receipt and the 436_447_170-byte case fixture — sit above the target and below the > ceiling, which is exactly the region a reduction is expected to occupy.

The figure draws the regression ceiling at 450,000,000 as a red dashed line, and it draws the 461,724,765-byte baseline point to the right of that line. 461,724,765 > 450,000,000, so the baseline is above the ceiling, not below it, and the figure is right while the caption is wrong. The receipt agrees with the figure: program-size/gate/receipt.json carries limits.300_cell_executable_bytes = 450000000 and rows[].before.executable_bytes = 461724765.

Second, the caption describes three points; the figure draws four. The fourth is 210,159,751 — "head state after mesh arrays (refused by the 50 MB threshold)" — and it is what the figure's own suptitle leads on ("its own 210 MB head state while the ceiling read 50 MB"). The record's paragraph silently drops it. Neither 436447170 nor 210159751 appears anywhere in program-size/gate/receipt.json, so two of the four values are unre-derivable from the named artifact.

Repair: correct the sentence to say the baseline sits above the ceiling, add the head state, and name the JSON path each byte count is read from. Score 42/100 — −30 caption contradicted by its own figure, −16 omitted fourth point, −12 two values in no receipt.

D4 — flux-arguments/flux-functions.png and profile-args/flux-functions.png are byte-identical duplicates

An md5 sweep of all 58 images returns exactly one duplicate pair, and it is these two (42,941 bytes each). Both are cited with the identical sentence about p′ (−16018.99, × 0.9) and FF′ (−0.19522, × 1.1).

Opening them, the arithmetic does check out on the pixels: the p′ panel draws committed at ≈ −16019 and scaled at ≈ −14417, and −16018.99 × 0.9 = −14417.09 ✓; the FF′ panel draws committed at ≈ −0.1952 and scaled at ≈ −0.2147, and −0.19522 × 1.1 = −0.214742 ✓. Neither has a receipt — no flux-arguments/.json and no profile-args/.json exists — so the source coefficients rest on the caption alone. Repair: keep one copy, repoint the other citation, emit the coefficient receipt. Score 58/100 each.

D5 — strict-exit-incidence.png plots a measurement that was never persisted, and does not say so in its caption

The two upper panels are titled "member-specific strict exit incidence" with a y-axis "strict qualification firing trip" running 1→16 — and every one of the 14 markers sits on the dashed "not persisted" line above the axis top. There is not one measured incidence value in either panel. The caption reads only "Paired width-1 warm solves with and without the strict settled exit, one compiled program per member" and discloses nothing. nova/AGENTS.md names the uniform-column refusal for precisely this shape.

To the figure's credit the missing members are disclosed in its own suptitle, and that re-derives digit-exact from strict-exit-incidence.json: completion.declared_member_count = 17, completion.missing_member_count = 3, 14 drawn. The DIII-D latency panel draws a connecting line between two categorical members, inviting a trend reading between two unrelated shots. Repair: drop the incidence panels until the value is persisted, or state "not persisted for any member" in the caption; remove the categorical connecting line. Score 55/100.

In width-one-compiled.png the value is 2.7501, which would fit comfortably under the same 25-unit axis and is still not drawn, so this is a renderer that never plots that series rather than an axis-limit problem.

D7 — scan-prototype/per-trip-residual-cpu.svg and -cuda.svg differ only in a timestamp

Both are 45,722 bytes. Diffing every text literal returns exactly one differing line, the render timestamp (2026-09-15T00:35:42.339861 against 2026-09-15T00:56:43.722605). Every plotted value, label and title is identical. Two files named cpu and cuda presenting the same numbers is either a rendering bug that wrote the CPU data twice or a genuine finding (the backends agree) that nothing states. Neither is cited; the receipts live under scan-prototype/parts/{cpu,cuda}-comparison.json, which the figures do not name. Repair: re-render each from its own receipt and establish which it is. Score 35/100 each.

D10 — three near-identical uncaptioned figures across three trip-quantum subdirectories

trip-quantum/outer-loop/outer-loop-trace.png, trip-quantum/fallback-carry/per-trip-after.png and trip-quantum/recovery-frozen/per-trip.png carry the identical suptitle ("Every compiled outer iteration executes; inactive bodies skip"), the identical second title ("Inner Newton work per active outer iteration"), and a bottom panel that is the same in all three — promotion attempts 12 across six indices, accepted promotions 12, 12, 12, 12, 5, 0. Only the top panel differs, and only in the first two bars (≈3.3 s in outer-loop, ≈0.2 s in the other two).

That agreement is legitimate: the three receipts were captured at three separate times (2026-09-04T22:35, 2026-09-05T01:35, 2026-09-05T04:07) and all three record attempted_promotions 12, accepted_promotions 12, accepted_newton_promotions 65 of attempted_newton_promotions 84. The counts genuinely did not move. The defect is that all three are published, none is cited, none is captioned, and nothing tells a reader what distinguishes them or that the repeated panel is a repeated result rather than a copied file.

All three also place the lower panel's title immediately under the upper panel's axis, where it reads as that axis's label. Repair: cite one with a caption naming the three captures and what changed between them, or fold the three into one figure with a third arm; move the lower title. Score 45/100 each.

D11 — hessenberg-trace/condition-trace.png contains no condition trace

The file is named condition-trace.png under trip-quantum/hessenberg-trace/. The figure is a three-panel bar comparison of "Exact Jacobi ratio" against "Constant discriminator" — warm device execution (380.1 vs 316.1 ms), host submissions (16,562 vs 15,074 CUDA launch API events) and host-side gaps (228.8 vs 183.2 ms). There is no condition number and no trace anywhere in it. Its receipt, hessenberg-trace/profile.json, is 563 KB and is not named by anything. The figure is cited nowhere, so the filename is the only description a reader gets, and it describes something else. Repair: rename to what it shows, or render the condition trace the name promises. Score 42/100.

D8 — 27 of 58 figures are cited by nothing

A file under docs/figures/ is served at /nova/figures/... and PNG/SVG are counted in the reckon Figures index, so an uncited figure is published and browsable with no caption, no receipt pointer and no paragraph. Twenty-seven are in that state.

Severity is set by one member. program-size/compile-abort/panels/diverted-single-null-cells-500-across-revisions.png shows a solved state at terminal residual 2.092e-01: the ochre solved axis triangle sits against the outboard wall surrounded by three pinhead contour blobs, the ochre X marker sits on the inboard side inside the plasma, and the ochre contours bear no resemblance to the blue reference's clean single-null separatrix. It is a failed solve, correctly and honestly annotated as such inside the figure — and published unlabelled in a directory named millisecond-converged-solve, where a browsing reader meets it with no caption saying it did not converge. Repair: for each, cite it with a caption whose numbers re-derive from a named receipt, or delete it. Lead with 2.092e-01 and "did not converge" if that panel is kept.

What did not fail

Stated because a review that only lists failures is not a measurement:

  • Every poloidal panel obeys the axis-off rule. All nine parse with zero tick groups and none carries an axis, a spine or a gridline.
  • Every scalar field is unfilled line contours on a shared level array. None is a filled contour, a colour map or painted scatter, and each across-revisions panel draws solved and reference on one shared Wb level array and says so in its own subtitle.
  • request-set/read-request-decomposition.json is exemplary. Thirty-one distinct numbers in a long caption, every one present in the committed receipt at a named key, every one digit-exact, and every one legible in the rendered figure. It is the standard the rest of the directory should be held to.
  • The program-shapes regeneration really landed. The census receipt contains 152, 154, 1.66, 1.68 and no 3072 or 3328 anywhere — the retired swept-product capture is gone from the artifact, not merely from the prose, and the bars are drawn at the regenerated ratios.
  • cache-prewarm/lane-cache-reuse names its receipt, and both wall clocks (877 s, of which 591 s compiling) are present in that receipt. It is the only figure in the directory whose caption names the JSON it was drawn from.
  • strict-exit-incidence.png and per-trip-components.png each disclose their own incompleteness in the figure — missing members in one suptitle, an uncaptured trace region shaded and labelled in the other. Where these figures fail it is in the record around them, not in concealment by the renderer.

Follow-ons for the coordinator

  1. Ten subdirectories publish figures with no receipt at any path (diverted-oracle-census, flux-arguments, profile-args, evaluator, evaluator-design, compile-memory, loop-audit, solve-profile, per-trip-kernels, and all panels/). A render-time rule requiring a receipt beside every published figure would have caught D1, D4, D9 and #22 before they landed. Outside this node's scope.
  2. Only one figure in 58 (cache-prewarm/lane-cache-reuse) names the JSON path its numbers come from. Every one of D3, D9, D11 and half the table's deductions would be cheaper to catch if the caption convention required a named path.
  3. The 27-uncited count is likely repo-wide, not local: the reckon Figures index serves every PNG and SVG, so any figure directory can accumulate published orphans. A repo-level check that every served image is cited from at least one HTML document is worth a node of its own.
  4. House style is not applied. 57 of 58 figures are raw matplotlib defaults; only trip-quantum/per-trip-kernels/per-trip-components.png uses the nova.media imas-ink style. The plotting rules bind flux maps specifically and those are obeyed, so this is an observation rather than a rule breach — but it is why five separate figures lose points to occluding legends, colliding titles and clipped suptitles that a shared layout would fix.

Figure audit — docs/figures/gs-absolute-accuracy

Coverage

46 PNG and 16 SVG — all 62 opened with the image read tool and scored. The SVGs are 410 KB each and exceed the tool's 256 KB ceiling, so each was rasterised with rsvg-convert -w 1400 into the review fence and the raster opened; the rasters are retained under svg-raster/ as the evidence of that read. The four questions

Numbers — one receipt name, two mutually inconsistent censuses

solovev-certificate-production-route.json is cited by name as the certificate source by two documents that publish different censuses of different cases:

DocumentDiverted case citedQualifiedResidual ladder
docs/evidence/gs-absolute-accuracy-certificate.htmldiverted-single-null3 of 165.291e-15 / 5.627e-15 / 2.423e-17 / 3.951e-2
docs/evidence/archive/gs-absolute-accuracy-gates-landed.htmldiverted-jump-bearing2 of 162.380e-15 / 3.079e-2 / 8.994e-05 / 4.860e-19

Both re-derive to the digit — from different part receipts in the same production-route-parts/ directory (diverted-jump-bearing-production-route-.json gives 2.3799e-15 / 3.0786e-02 / 8.9936e-05 / 4.8598e-19; diverted-single-null-production-route-.json gives 5.2914e-15 / 5.6270e-15 / 2.4230e-17 / 3.9507e-02). One receipt name, two censuses, and a reader cannot tell which the named file holds.

The EFIT arm re-derives cleanly against the live documents and fails against the archive:

RowReceipt efit-reproduction.jsonmast-efit-match.html…gates-landed.html

All eleven live per-figure caption numbers re-derive exactly (e.g. 00000c4a7b:179 "1.883636 (gauge +1.919 Wb)" against rows[6].solve_against_label.value = 1.8836358240990305, additive_gauge_wb = 1.9188735440215905). Two live-document defects survive:

  • mast-efit-match.html lede: "Every solve-label fraction sits below the 0.0116 floor". Row 21983/35 is 0.01238834636377499 against the 0.011614703393987796 floor — above it, as that figure's own caption says.
  • mast-22086-row-43.png caption: "21978/35 is the paired arm". The receipt's rows[5]…basin_evidence.paired_arm reads "22086/43 pure" — the row names itself.

Every production-route caption number re-derives to the digit; the full check is in findings-solovev.json.

Rules — three painters in one directory, two of them banned

The 11 EFIT panels are filled diverging colour maps with a colorbar, carry full axes, spines, ticks, tick labels and R [m] / Z [m], draw no wall, and draw no null markers.

The 16 NaN-era Solov'ev panels and the 3 diverted-jump-bearing-production-route- panels are painted scatter on log colour scales with three colorbars, again with full axes and tick labels, a bare boundary curve, no wall and no nulls. Painted scatter is the representation the rule names as banned. That the jump-bearing *production-route panels use this painter — while the other sixteen production-route panels use the contour painter — means "production-route" does not identify the painter, and the three jump-bearing production-route figures are the qualified rows of the archive census.

The 16 production-route contour panels (and their 16 SVG companions) largely obey the rules — axis off, unfilled line contours, levels printed per panel, wall drawn, draw_nulls vocabulary — with two defects:

  • the top-left subtitle is clipped on all 32, and the PNG always clips more than its SVG companion: the single-null PNG reads "ytic blue contours…" where its SVG reads "analytic blue contours…" in full; the strong pair read "blue contours…" / "ytic blue…"; moderate "blue…" / "tic blue…"; weak "lue…" / "ic blue…". So the PNG and the SVG of one panel are not the same image, and the raster is the one the evidence documents embed. figure-panel-pin.json normalises three container fields and cannot see this.
  • diverted-single-null-production-route-{reduced,cells-300} draw no analytic X-point marker although the case family's reference is diverted, so "both null sets where a reference exists" is unmet.

Physics — four misrepresentations

  1. A limited state drawn and captioned as diverted. diverted-single-null-production-route-reduced.json and -cells-300.json both record exact_topology.class and root_topology.class = "limited". The figures are titled diverted-single-null, captioned "Diverted single-null", and draw a cusped boundary floating clear of the wall on every side. 4 entries (2 PNG + 2 SVG).
  1. Twelve limited states with a boundary that never touches the wall. All twelve moderate-rotation-conventional-static-, strong-rotation-compact-static- and weak-rotation-reactor-static-* production-route receipts carry class = "limited" in both topology reads, and on every panel the drawn boundary sits strictly inside the black wall with no contact point. Under the binding rule a limited boundary must touch the wall; a floating closed surface is fiction. 24 entries (12 PNG + 12 SVG).
  1. A diverted case drawn with a degenerate lens boundary and no wall. All seven diverted-jump-bearing panels (3 production-route + 4 NaN-era) record exact_topology.class = "diverted" yet draw a thin lens that cannot be the separatrix of a diverted equilibrium, with no wall at all — the four production-route receipts carry render_data.wall_units_rz_m of length 0. 7 entries.
  1. The DIII-D difference field is a monopole, not the captioned vertical displacement. All five DIII-D captions assert "a whole-plasma vertical displacement at roughly 0.3 of span". Frames 179 and 44 are sign-definite — positive over nearly the whole domain with one negative core near the axis. Frames 146 and 137 do show the antisymmetric dipole, as does every MAST panel, which is what makes the difference legible. 2 entries.

Honesty

  • All four diverted-jump-bearing NaN-era psi panels label their colourbar "absolute psi error" while the bar runs negative over its whole range (−0.5 to −1.2, −0.6 to −1.4, −0.5 to −1.2, −0.5 to −1.0). An absolute error cannot be negative.
  • strong-rotation-compact-static-cells-1000.png: the psi panel plots scatter only in a narrow band |Z| < 0.2 while its own boundary reaches |Z| = 0.4 — roughly 60% of the support is simply not drawn, with nothing saying so. The gradient and Hessian panels of the same figure cover the full ellipse.
  • diverted-jump-bearing-production-route-cells-500.png: a wedge of the psi scatter is missing near the lower-left boundary with no mask legend.
  • The twelve static production-route panels render gradient and Hessian panels that are visually near-empty — one small cluster on an otherwise blank panel — beside captions reporting axis errors up to 64.3 mm.
  • production-route-parts/diverted-jump-bearing-production-route-cells-300.json exists with no figure; the other three rungs have one. The three jump-bearing production-route figures are cited by no document, so their numbers are uncheckable by construction.
  • efit/diiid-d3d_shot_0003ff34e7-frame-44.png draws a smooth finite field for a row recording finite: false, fixed_point_residual: null, with nothing on the panel marking it.
  • The DIII-D colour range is set by a single saturating corner cell on all five frames, which is why the reported RMS "exceeds the figure's plotted range" — the document names the symptom, not the cause.

Coverage caveat, stated plainly. The node's evidence fence asks for every one of the 114 figures opened with the image read tool and scored. Within the 55-minute fence 20 were opened and scored; the remaining 94 were not individually opened. What does cover all 114 is the structural pass — the citation map, the SVG axis-furniture scan and the receipt census in the table above, all machine-derived over the whole directory. The 20 were chosen to cover every distinct rendering template in the directory, so the template-level findings below generalise, but a per-figure score for the other 94 has not been earned and is not asserted. See follow_ons.

Findings that hold across the whole directory

F1 — 40 of 114 figures are cited nowhere. A figure no document references cannot be checked by a reader against a claim, and the Numbers and Physics questions are unanswerable for it because there is no caption or paragraph to re-derive. Whole sub-directories are dark: all 4 reposed-fixture SVGs, all 3 topology-ladder, all 3 vertex-census, all 4 private-region, both spline-read, both gate-b, both first-order-coupling, 3 of 4 read-representation, all 4 wholecell-rows cells-2500 panels. Repair: cite each from the evidence record that motivated it, or delete it.

F2 — exact-clip-memory/base-memory-scaling.svg is hand-drawn, not rendered. 1 kB of literal SVG: <path d="M177 249L441 148L713 77"/> with hand-placed <text> labels 25.47, 51.81, 70.41. It is not a product of the committed nova.media stack and nothing binds its geometry to a receipt. Its own drawn geometry disagrees with its own labels: reading the marker centres against its hand-drawn tick scale gives 25.6, 51.1 and 69.1 GiB against the printed 25.47, 51.81 and 70.41 — third-digit disagreement at the second and third points. Repair: regenerate from the receipt with the project plotting stack.

F3 — the solve-panel template omits half its null vocabulary. The 2x2 template shared by cold-seed, gate-c-resolve, exact-clip-seed, wholecell-rows, limited-shadow/solve-panels, exact-clip-memory panels and rung-a-certificate obeys the rules well on axes (off), contours (unfilled lines), stated levels (printed under each error panel) and the wall (closed black outline on every panel). It fails the null rule in two ways: the three error panels draw no null markers at all, and on several rows only ONE of the two null sets appears in the comparison panel even though a reference is present. Hollow markers for other qualified nulls never appear anywhere in the directory.

F4 — the magnetic axis is drawn as a plain dot in four figures. zero-residual, read-representation, rung-a and limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow mark the axis with a red circle instead of the draw_nulls solid triangle, so the same quantity carries two glyphs across one directory.

Itemised deductions

19. gate-c-resolve/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-1000.png — 38

Receipts: docs/figures/cut-cell-current-attribution/gate-c-resolve/*.json. Cited by docs/evidence/gs-absolute-accuracy-certificate.html.

  • −35 physics. Title reads diverted-single-null · cells-1000 · unqualified · residual 1.119e-01 · converged False. The analytic (blue) field is a proper single null: a closed separatrix passing through a blue X at the bottom. The solved (ochre) field shown beside it is a different equilibrium — its nested contours close on an ochre axis triangle sitting hard against the OUTBOARD WALL, far outside the blue separatrix, and no solved X-point is marked anywhere. The solved state has no separatrix and no saddle. A reader who takes the panel as "the diverted single null at 1000 cells" reads a diverted equilibrium; the drawn solved field is not one. Repair: either re-render at a converged state, or add to the caption, in the title line beside converged False, that the solved field carries no admitted saddle and its axis lies outside the analytic boundary.
  • −15 rules. Only the analytic null set is drawn for the saddle; the repo rule requires both null sets where a reference exists, and requires every flux map to mark its admitted X-point. Repair: draw the solved null set, or mark explicitly that none was admitted.
  • −12 honesty. unqualified · converged False is present and correct, but nothing in the panel tells the reader the solved topology differs in kind, not degree.

17. wholecell-rows/panels/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-1000.png — 45

  • −35 physics. residual=1.198e-01 · converged=no. The ochre solved axis triangle sits on the OUTBOARD EDGE of the blue separatrix, not inside it — the solved contours do not close on the axis the panel marks. The ochre X does coincide with the blue X, so the saddle is found while the axis is not, which is the harder failure to see and the one the figure presents without comment. Repair: as above.
  • −20 honesty. Same shape as #19.

20. limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow.png — 30

  • −30 rules/honesty. The figure carries no title, no caption, no legend and no text of any kind. Four distinct glyph families are drawn (red dot, red ×, blue triangle, a purple marker cluster) and none is identified. Nothing states the fixture, the cell count, the residual or the converged flag.
  • −20 physics. A blue triangle — the draw_nulls magnetic-axis glyph — is placed below the X-point, down in the divertor leg region, where a magnetic axis cannot be. Either the vocabulary is being reused for a contact point, which actively misleads a reader who knows the vocabulary, or the read is wrong. Repair: caption the figure, and use a distinct glyph for the contact point.
  • −20. The purple marker cluster, which appears to be the thing the figure exists to show, is unexplained.

16. zero-residual/panels/zero-residual-map.png — 48

  • −25 physics/honesty. The bottom-left panel is titled moderate-rotation-conventional-static terminal flux (persisted) and draws no flux contours at all — a red boundary ring and a red dot on an otherwise empty panel. The top-left panel of the same figure does carry contours, so this is not a style choice. A panel captioned "terminal flux" that contains no flux is a claim the drawing does not support. Repair: establish whether the persisted array is flat or absent and either render it or replace the panel with a stated absence.
  • −15 rules. Magnetic axis drawn as a red dot, not the draw_nulls triangle; no X-point vocabulary anywhere.
  • −12 numbers. No residual and no converged flag in the title or any subtitle, against the repo rule that every forward-state evidence panel carries both.

18. rung-a/weak-booked-minus-analytic.png — 42

  • −20 honesty/legibility. The caption weak: booked-minus-analytic per-cell current [A] and the level line flux levels -1.55e+00..7.90e+00 Wb; current levels -2.75e+04..4.41e+02 A are drawn on top of the plot, overlapping the red boundary contour, and are partly illegible. The stated levels are the only numbers the figure carries and they cannot be read reliably.
  • −15 rules. Axis drawn as a red dot, not the triangle.
  • −7. No legend: blue contours, dashed-orange contours, ochre hexagons and the red ring are four distinct objects with no key, and no residual or converged flag.

15. limited-shadow/solve-panels/chord/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-500.png — 52

  • −20 numbers. The title is diverted-single-null · cells-500 · qualified — it carries no residual and no converged flag, uniquely among every sibling of this template, all of which print both. Repair: restore the two fields the template already emits elsewhere.
  • −18 honesty. The absolute psi error panel is filled edge to edge with dense closed contours on levels 4.72e-20, 1.14e-17 … 7.92e-17 — machine round-off. The panel draws a rich, structured error field that is entirely floating-point noise, and nothing labels it as such. Beside it the gradient and hessian panels are nearly empty at 1e-5 and 1e-3, so the psi panel is the most visually striking of the three and the least meaningful. Repair: either state the round-off floor on the panel, as limited-shadow/oracle-overlay-error.png correctly does, or suppress contouring below it.
  • −10 rules. Only the solved null set is drawn; the analytic boundary contour is drawn without its axis triangle.

14. read-representation/single-null-exact-representations.png — 55

  • −15 rules. Magnetic axis as a red dot, not the triangle.
  • −15 rules/physics. A single-null fixture whose X-point is plainly visible at the bottom vertex of the black separatrix, with no X-point marker drawn.
  • −10 legibility. The caption runs under the plot and overlaps the drawn hexagons at the right, so it is read across the figure content.
  • −5 layout. The drawing occupies only the right ~55% of the canvas; the left ~45% is blank.
  • Numbers unverifiable: the directory holds 4 figures and 1 JSON, and this figure is cited nowhere, so the 1.0% pitch threshold in the caption has no named receipt path to re-derive from.

13. exact-clip-seed/panels/weak-rotation-reactor-static-110-comparison.png — 66

  • −18 numbers. Title is weak-rotation-reactor-static · 110 requested cells only — no residual, no converged flag, though the sibling *-terminals.png template prints both.
  • −12 rules. Only the solved (ochre) axis triangle; the analytic reference is drawn as a blue contour with no null of its own.
  • −4. Level array is stated on the right panel and readable — this part passes.

12. positional-stiffness/figures/translated-40mm.png — 68

  • −15 numbers. No residual and no converged flag in any of the six subtitles.
  • −10 numbers. No level array is stated on any panel, and the subtitle does not claim shared levels, so the analytic/translated comparison cannot be checked for a shared physical level array.
  • −7. Rows mix resolutions (row 1 is 110 cells, rows 2–3 are 300) without the grid saying so structurally; a reader must read each subtitle to notice.
  • Rules otherwise pass: axis off, wall drawn, both null sets present, unfilled line contours.

11. exact-clip-low-state/panels/weak-static-110-terminals.png — 68

  • −20 numbers. Both right-hand panels state fixed levels [-0.1, -0.01, … 0.001, 0.01, 0. — the level array is truncated mid-number by the panel width. The stated levels are the figure's own claim about its contours and they are not fully readable. Repair: wrap the level line or reduce its font.
  • −12 rules. Only the ochre null set drawn.
  • Residual 3.666e-13 / 3.420e-13 with converged=True are present and correct.

10. census-kernel/census-kernel-timing.png — 70

  • −10. Cited by no document (F1).
  • −10 legibility. The ms whole-solve target annotation collides with the 1.4 ms point label at the left of the left panel.
  • −10 numbers. The right panel's bar value labels (13 9 26, 550 1119 2756, 1361 5092 30650) are counts, printed over an axis whose units are marginal seconds per batch; two quantities share one visual channel with no key.
  • Left panel reads cleanly: kernel 0.0489 / 0.0521 / 0.0537 ms against production read 1.4 / 4.9 / 30 ms — a legitimate log-axis line chart, so axes and gridlines are correct here (the no-axes rule governs 2D poloidal panels, not 1D traces).

9. gate-c-resolve/weak-rotation-reactor-static-production-route-cells-1000.png — 72

  • −16 honesty. absolute psi error levels run to 5.29e+01 — a 53 Wb absolute flux error, drawn across the whole interior with no comment anywhere in the figure on whether that is expected at converged False.
  • −12 physics. Blue and ochre axes are visibly separated; the state is not converged and the caption says so, which is why this is a deduction and not a misrepresentation.

8. limiter-subpanel/limiter-wall-contact-read.png — 72

  • −10. Cited by no document, and its directory holds zero JSON receipts, so no number in it can be re-derived — the Numbers question is unanswerable, not failed.
  • −8 rules. Magnetic axis drawn as a green triangle — right shape, off-vocabulary colour.
  • −10. Right panel legend keys six series by fixture and quantity but the left panel's red circle-in-square contact glyph appears in no key.
  • Physics passes and is worth saying so: the blue separatrix is drawn tangent to the black wall at the marked contact point — a limited boundary touching the wall, exactly the thing the rule asks a limited figure to show.

7. oracle-start/diverted-single-null-cells-1000-fixture_exterior_control.png — 78

  • −12 rules. The analytic reference is drawn as a blue separatrix with no blue nulls; only the ochre triangle and ochre × appear. Both sets are required where a reference exists.
  • −10. Cited by no document (F1).
  • Physics passes: residual=7.823e-19; converged=True, and the drawn diverted boundary passes through the marked X-point. This is the strongest physics in the directory.

6. xpoint-cell/carrier-vs-independent-cells-110.png — 80

  • −15 communication. The title claims a distinction — "the clip's core wedge is the quad beside the saddle; the reconstruction's confined sector is the quad above it" — but the right panel draws its confined sector as a hairline light-grey line that is nearly invisible at the rendered size. The figure exists to show a difference the reader cannot see. Repair: raise the weight and contrast of the reconstruction sector.
  • −5. No scale or cell-pitch reference on either panel.

3–5. cold-seed/{weak,moderate,strong}-*-production-route-reduced.png — 80 each

  • −12 honesty. All three are converged False (residuals 1.252e-02, 7.831e-03, 9.812e-03) and all three print it, which is correct. The deduction is that the solved ochre contours ring violently at the outboard midplane — visible high-frequency oscillation crossing the boundary — and nothing in the caption tells the reader that this structure is an artefact of a non-converged state rather than a feature of the field.
  • −8 rules. Blue and ochre axis triangles are coincident at this scale and the ochre one covers the blue; a reader cannot confirm both null sets are drawn.
  • Everything else passes: axis off, wall closed on all four panels, unfilled line contours, level arrays stated under every error panel, shared Wb levels declared.

2. limited-shadow/oracle-overlay-error.png — 86

  • −8 rules. Row 4 (single-null 500 control) draws only the ochre null set; the analytic reference gets no nulls.
  • −6. Orange carries two meanings in one figure: "solved ψ / nulls" in column 1 and "negative Δψ" in column 2, both keyed to one legend entry.
  • Credited, and it is the reason this figure scores high: it prints max |Δψ|/span 2.099e-01 · not-oracle-matched beside residual 1.39e-15 · converged yes, and its footnote states outright that "a converged row is a self-consistent fixed point, not an oracle match". A converged state that is 21% off the oracle is exactly the thing a shoddy figure would let a reader misread, and this one refuses to. Column 3 is a 1D log trace, so its axes and gridlines are correct.

1. limited-shadow/lfs-contours-before-after.png — 92

  • −5. The legend keys five series but not the black wall.
  • −3 numbers. Each row declares "one analytic Wb level array in all three columns" but does not print the array values, so a reader can confirm sharing but not the levels.
  • Credited: every one of the nine subtitles prints its own residual and converged flag (2.00e-03/no, 1.39e-15/yes, n/a/reference, …); the wall is closed on all nine panels; the axis triangle is drawn on all nine; and the footnote says which row is missing and why — "the weak 2500 reproduction persisted only its 54-carrier census, not its terminal flux array, so that row is not drawn". That is the honesty question answered without being asked. This figure is the template the rest of the directory should be repaired toward.

COMPLETE COVERAGE — all 114 figures opened and scored

The coverage caveat above is superseded. Every one of the 114 PNG and SVG files was opened with the image read tool and scored. The 37 SVGs were rendered to PNG with rsvg-convert -w 1400 -b white and opened as images, so the reviewer saw what a reader sees, not the XML.

Full ranked table: per-figure-table.md. Machine-readable: scores.json.

The eleven physics misrepresentations

  1. gate-c-resolve/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-1000.png (38) and
  2. gate-c-resolve/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-500.png (38) — both converged False (1.119e-01, 1.938e-01). The solved field closes on an axis pressed against the outboard wall, outside the analytic separatrix, with no solved X-point marked at all. Neither has a separatrix or an admitted saddle. Both are captioned diverted-single-null; the 500 is cited by three documents including gs-absolute-accuracy-certificate.html.
  3. wholecell-rows/panels/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-1000.png (45) — the solved axis sits on the outboard edge of the analytic separatrix while the saddle is found, so the contours do not close on the axis the panel marks.
  4. wholecell-rows/panels/weak-rotation-reactor-static-production-route-cells-2500.png (40) — title reads residual=0.000e+00 · converged=yes. An exactly zero residual, printed to three decimals, beside its own psi-error panel whose levels run to 3.17e+01 Wb. A state cannot be an exact fixed point and carry a 31.7 Wb flux error against the analytic. This is a claim about the instrument: the residual reported is not the residual of the state drawn. It is the most serious single finding in the directory because a zero reads as the best row in the bank.
  5. rung-a-certificate/diverted-single-null-production-route-reduced.png (40) — residual 6.354e-01 · converged False, and the solved X-point glyph is drawn at the INBOARD MIDPLANE, nowhere near the analytic X at the bottom, with the solved axis up in the top-right of the plasma. A directory named rung-a-certificate certifying a state whose saddle is in the wrong place by half a machine radius.
  6. limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow.png (30) — no text of any kind, and the draw_nulls magnetic-axis triangle placed in the divertor leg.
  7. zero-residual/panels/zero-residual-map.png (48) — a panel titled "terminal flux (persisted)" that draws no flux.
  8. gate-b/absolute-flux-error.svg (45) — six poloidal panels whose title claims "wall drawn; axis and X-point marked". Panels 1 and 3 are empty except the wall and an axis dot, while their own subtitles quote max |error| 0.9752 Wb = 0.0168 span and 0.00272 Wb = 0.0197 span. The contour levels are set above the stated maximum, so a nonzero error is drawn as a blank panel. Axis is a red dot, not the triangle.
  9. cut-cell-coupling/weak-110-coupling-errors.svg (50) — the middle panel is titled before as built, rms 0.00000 of span and is empty, between zeroth only, 0.00603 and after clipped, 0.00027. An rms of exactly zero to five decimals, drawn as nothing, in the middle of a before/after sequence that then reads as the change making things worse. The zero is an instrument artefact presented as a result.
  10. positional-stiffness/figures/weak-rotation-reactor-static-cells-110-residual.png (45) and 11. ...cells-300-residual.png (45) — the two figures are titled 110 requested cells and 300 requested cells and are pixel-identical: every marker, every curve, every legend entry. Two different discretisations cannot produce the same curve; one of the two is mis-titled or the driver rendered the same array twice. The figures assert a resolution dependence they do not contain.

Further findings from the full pass

F5 — three figure pairs are duplicate renders. newton-geometry/-production-route-reduced.png is pixel-identical to its -chord_live-reduced.png sibling except that the title has had chord_live, the residual and the trip count stripped — so the "production route" figure of each of the three fixtures is the chord_live figure with its evidence removed (weak 1.630e-03/6 trips, moderate 1.490e-03/7, strong 1.096e-02/7). Scored 50 each. xpoint-cell/single-null-wedges-cells-300 and carrier-vs-independent-cells-110 each exist as both PNG and SVG of the same figure.

F6 — gate-c-resolve resolved nothing. All fourteen figures in the directory are unqualified · converged False, residuals 3.79e-02 to 1.94e-01.

F7 — the residual/converged line is missing from five templates. exact-clip-seed (all 12), exact-clip-memory solve panels, limited-shadow/solve-panels/chord (all 4), positional-stiffness/translated-40mm, read-representation (all 4) and rung-a (all 3) print no residual and no converged flag, while gate-c-resolve, wholecell-rows, cold-seed, rung-a-certificate and newton-geometry/chord_live print both from what is plainly the same renderer.

F8 — moment-order and gate-b print a leading minus on the cell count — -110 cells, -300 cells, in all six figures.

F9 — the xpoint-cell wedge panels draw the two null sets as an hourglass: an ochre down-triangle over a blue up-triangle. A downward triangle is not the draw_nulls axis glyph, so the coincident-axis case is rendered as a marker that means something else.

Correction to F2. The provenance finding stands — base-memory-scaling.svg is hand-authored, not a nova.media render, and nothing binds its marks to a receipt. The third-digit disagreement claimed above was my pixel arithmetic on hand-placed coordinates, which is not a reliable instrument; opened as an image the labels and marks read consistently. Score 60, on provenance alone.

Figure audit — docs/figures/playable-forward-solve (plan: playable-forward-solve)

Reviewer node fsa-audit-figures-playable-solve, plan nova:figure-and-solver-audit §1. Worktree .reckon-worktrees/nova-a0f1e0938fc2/s19-codex-20260916/fsa-audit-figures-playable-solve, base 95fd0972de0ea4f83c1feba2036662c8a7c4b41c. Nothing under nova/ was committed and no figure was regenerated.

Coverage

The directory holds 60 image files (55 PNG + 5 SVG) across 26 non-empty sub-directories, beside 111 JSON receipts. All 60 were opened with the image read tool and scored. Where a receipt exists, every number in the figure and in the paragraph citing it was re-derived by JSON path and the agreement or disagreement is stated in the row.

Three of the five SVGs (constraint-globalisation.svg, compensator-jacobian-response.svg, multi-trip-target.svg) are matplotlib output and return as XML rather than a raster, so they were audited from their path data — a weaker read, marked per row. The other two (h200-playable-cache-refusal.svg, shape-regularisation-curve.svg) are hand-authored SVG and were read in full as source, which is the stronger read for them because their data are in the markup.

Scored table

Directory summary

  • Figures in directory: 60 (55 PNG, 5 SVG). Scored: 60.
  • Mean score: 55.4.
  • Scoring under 70: 43 of 60.
  • Misrepresenting physics at any score: 17 — the seven limited-anchor rows; boundary-routing/area-evidence; the three reversed-current-guard panels; early-frame-placement; the three rotation solovev-certificate chord panels titled "qualified" over an 11–12 % relative flux error; all-prescribed-negative-control, whose control beats its own arm; and shape-regularisation-curve.svg, whose admissible region is empty at every weight.
  • Figures whose directory carries no receipt JSON at all, so no number re-derives: reversed-current-guard/ (3 figures) and view/ (2 figures). A sixth, shape-regularisation-curve.svg, has a receipt for its x axis and none for its curves.
  • Best figures: h200-playable-cache-refusal.svg (90), response-comparison.png (88), chord/diverted-single-null… (86), the two parallel-components wall plots (86 each). Only three figures in the directory obey all four plotting rules by construction: response-comparison.png, chord/diverted-single-null…, and mast-22086-43-bulk-z-axis-component.png.
  • Worst figures: the two byte-identical *-throughput.png keyframes panels (20 each), which contain no data at all — two fence lines and a legend; boundary-routing/area-evidence/boundary-wall-proximity.png (25); the seven limited-anchor rows (28–34); the three topology-batch panels (30 each), whose figure title is a bare comma-separated integer list.
  • Byte-identical duplicate: keyframes/h200-playable-axis-continuation-throughput.png and keyframes/h200-press-stage-throughput.png (md5 0957ec23a73a7e8ef13eac7742b18133).
  • Panels drawn with no data or with an unreadable title: 9 — centroid-attribution right panel (empty), terminal-state-parity "element 0" (empty series), the three reversed-current-guard middle panels (empty comparison), the two *-throughput.png keyframes figures (entirely empty), and the clipped titles on the seven limited-anchor rows, the three topology-batch panels and signed-error-summaries.
  • Of the 60 images, 27 are cited by a document (playable-forward-solve-landed.html 18 + 2 boundary-routing, poloidal-convergence-atlas.html 7, playable-forward-solve.html 2); 33 are orphans that no evidence record or plan references — including all four solovev-certificate chord panels, which are terminal forward states.

Coverage note

Nothing remains unopened. The first 36 rows were scored inside the original 55-minute fence and the remaining 24 on the coordinator's return of the node; the scoring standard is the same in both passes and the directory summary above is computed over all 60.

Full re-review of the cut-cell current attribution method

Node cca-full-re-review-of-the-method, run r-20260921T054301079853-cca-full-re-review-of-the-method, worktree base 0322e911a52588e116bb76a2243ff0dc728cc655, plan nova:cut-cell-current-attribution at plan version 134.

Status: the ordered review is COMPLETE. The moment representation was treated first and to the depth the fence asks, with three measurements on one all_debug allocation each, two figures, and a named verdict. All 11 plan sections, 5 locked decisions and all 8 sections of the evidence record carry evidence-backed verdicts. The first pass left §3, §6 and §7 unverified; a second pass closed them, and in doing so overturned two of this review's own findings — see §4. Nothing below is asserted from a figure or a receipt I did not read, and where the second pass overturned a first-pass claim of mine the claim is withdrawn in place rather than quietly edited away.

0. The headline, before the sections

The lead's suspicion is that the method leans too hard on the cut-cell current gradients, and that the low-field-side island chain is that overreach made visible. The first half is refuted by measurement; the second half is confirmed, with the mechanism sharpened rather than replaced.

  • The gradient term is not too strong. Dropping it is worse in 152 of 152 measured rows — every clipped fraction, both cut orientations, at the neighbouring cell centroids and at the cell's own vertices alike. At clipped fraction 0.01 the zeroth-plus-first image is 4.4x closer to the exact clipped polygon than zeroth-only; at fraction 0.5 it is 895x closer.
  • The gradient term's residual is misplaced, and that is the artifact. Its error is 4.6x larger at the cell's own vertices (worst rms relative flux error 0.1694) than at the neighbour centroids (0.0367), with a signed peak-to-peak spread of 0.489 of the mean own-vertex flux. The own vertices are where the carrier reads the flux and where the locked vertex-ring census looks for four cyclic sign changes. The evidence record's 4.39-fold squared-flux-error enrichment sits on these same outboard cut cells, so the residual and the artifact are co-located — which is consistency, not proof of cause, and finding 1 asks for the overlay that would settle it.
  • The representation that keeps the frozen interaction matrix without the artifact is a bounded-shift rule: clamp the delivered first moment so the supported-centroid offset never exceeds Ixx/(A*R) = 5R/24 = 0.2083 R, paired with the order-two moments the moment-order receipt already adjudicated. It keeps the matrix frozen and cached, costs one elementwise saturation per cell, changes no array shape, and gives up accuracy only at small clipped fraction where the cell's own current is proportional to that fraction. Full adjudication of all four fence candidates in section 3.
  • A second outboard mechanism is larger and independent of the representation. The unit-amplitude census puts a 5-to-7 percent current deficit on the outboard midplane: against an analytic 16.315 MA, whole-cell booking books 15.541 MA (0.953) and the exact clip 15.149 MA (0.929), and both drop the same cells — 106 (fully inside, 192 kA), 104, 108, 83 and 116 (116–192 kA each) — because candidacy comes from the participation label, not the clip geometry. The bounded-shift remedy does nothing about it, and §6 rung A already specifies its fix and is unlanded at 0 attempts. Rank the two before building either.

0.1 A correction to this review's own first reading

I first read the committed cold-seed panel for weak-rotation-reactor-static as showing the chain on an interior outboard column, and ranked "the chain is not where the cut cells are" as the top finding. The evidence record's census contradicts that reading and outranks it. Evidence record §1, on the 500-cell weak row:

> the outboard window R 7.25 to 7.75 m, Z 0 to 0.5 m holds 1.44 percent of nodes > and 6.33 percent of squared flux error, a 4.39-fold enrichment, and the same > patch appears on a rectangular tiling at matched resolution (enrichment 4.32), > so the site is the clip-and-moment path the two tilings share and not the kernel > or the tiling

with the polygon-analytic kernel exonerated separately at packed-to-scalar relative rms 3e-11 to 1.2e-10 and no correlation of kernel error with cut cells. Cells 101 and 102 sit at R about 7.79 m — the same window.

That is a quantified enrichment from a named receipt, reproduced across two tilings. Mine was a position judged by eye from a panel drawn with the axis off, correctly per the repository's plotting rules — exactly the reading a panel without axes cannot support. The localization stands as the plan states it: the artifact is on the outboard boundary-cut cells.

Two things from that first reading do survive and are worth one cheap measurement:

  • The cold-seed row is unconverged — terminal_fixed_point_residual 0.014726, converged false, termination active_set_settled after 6 trips, and magnetic_axis_position_error_m 0.068664 against the committed 0.001846, 37x worse — so whether its chain sits where the converged row's does is not established, and an arrested iteration can put structure where the converged state does not.
  • The amplitude pin is exonerated on that row: seed_amplitude 1.0122, terminal_amplitude 0.9533, both near unity. The parallel diagnostic node reached the same conclusion by a different route.

The general lesson is the actionable one, and it is finding 1: the plan's strongest localization number lives in prose while the panels a reader looks at carry no axes, so localization gets judged by eye. Put the cut-cell census and the enrichment window on the panel.

Read §4 before acting on §2 or §3. A second pass over the evidence record closed §3, §6 and §7, withdrew two of this review's own findings, and independently confirmed the central measurement. The verdict below is unchanged by it and better supported.

1. The moment representation, derived

1.1 What the code at head actually delivers

ForwardFluxOperator.coupling_current_moments (nova/equilibrium/forward_operator.py:2942-2973) converts the physical first moments to density coefficients by inverting the atomic cell's area-normalised second-moment tensor:

determinant = radial_second * vertical_second - cross_second**2
radial   = (vertical_second * moments.radial_moment - cross_second * moments.vertical_moment) / determinant
vertical = (radial_second * moments.vertical_moment - cross_second * moments.radial_moment) / determinant

second is self.moment_geometry.second_moment, the atomic polygon's moments — §9 of the plan is the not-yet-landed section that would make this the clipped polygon's. So for a cut cell the frozen blocks are handed the linear density

rho(x) = Q/A + (I^-1 M) . (x - x_c)     over the WHOLE atomic cell

whose zeroth and first moments equal the clipped sliver's, and whose support is the entire cell.

1.2 The cell is a regular hexagon, and the probe reproduces it exactly

The first-order-coupling receipt records cell 30 of the weak-rotation-reactor 110-cell row with area_m2 = 0.21721510247471887 and second_moments_m2 = 0.017417944150809855. Those are not square: they are the regular hexagon, for which I/A = 5R^2/24. The probe rebuilds the hexagon from the area alone and reproduces the receipt's area to 2.56e-15 relative and its area-normalised second moment to 1.39e-15 relative — the geometry positive control, asserted in the driver rather than eyeballed. A second control checks that the matched density really does carry the sliver's moments over the atomic support: 1.00e-13 on the current and 1.53e-12 on the radial moment across all 108 rows. (The vertical-moment control reads 6.5e14 and is a denominator artifact, not a failure — the vertical moment is exactly zero by symmetry in half the rows and the guard divides by 1e-30. Reported, not dropped.)

1.3 The density a full cell carries when its moments match a sliver

Measured over 54 clipped fractions x 2 cut orientations (moment-representation-receipt.json):

clipped fractionoffset / Rdensity min / meandensity max / mean
0.010.918-3.41+5.41
0.050.817-2.92+4.92
0.100.742-2.56+4.56
0.200.635-2.05+4.05
0.500.389-0.87+2.87
0.720.220-0.054+2.05
0.980.018+0.913+1.087

(cut normal through a vertex; the edge-mid-point orientation is in the receipt and in the figure and differs only in where the far extreme sits.)

The clipped fraction below which the representation stops being a centroid shift and becomes a dipole source is 0.72 for a cut normal through a vertex and 0.68 for a cut normal through an edge mid-point — the fractions at which the matched density first changes sign inside the cell. 41 of 54 and 39 of 54 sampled fractions are below it. Below that fraction the cell is not a blob moved sideways; it is a monopole plus a dipole that is larger than the monopole, and by fraction 0.01 the density swings across nearly nine times its own mean.

This reproduces, by an independent route, the 5R/24 = 0.2083 R offset bound the parallel diagnostic node derived analytically the same morning (reports/.../cca-which-mechanism-puts-current-on-the-low-field-side/linear-moment-dipole-bound.txt). My vertex-cut sign change appears between offset 0.220 R (min = -0.054) and the next sample up; the edge-cut sign change needs a larger offset because its far extreme is an edge mid-point at sqrt(3)R/2, not a vertex at R. The two orientations bracket the analytic bound. Two instruments, one number.

1.4 The near field: where the reconciliation with the diagnostic node lies

The diagnostic node concludes from the same bound that the dipole delivery is the surviving mechanism. My near-field measurement does not support that as stated, and this is the one place the two records differ.

Imaging the exact clipped polygon and the matched density through the axisymmetric poloidal-flux Green's function (complete elliptic integrals, degree-6 triangle rule on a 4-level subdivision):

siteworst first-order rms rel. errorworst zeroth-only rms rel. errorrows where first-order is worse
six neighbour centroids0.03670.16990 of 108
the cell's own six vertices0.16940.30540 of 44

The dipole, however violent it looks as a density, is a valid second-order representation of the moment pair in the field it radiates. Removing it makes every measured number worse. So "the gradients lean too hard" is refuted.

What survives, and what I believe the diagnostic node is actually seeing, is the 4.6x concentration of the residual at the cell's own nodes (0.1694 against 0.0367) with a signed spread of 0.489 of the mean own-vertex flux. That is a read defect, not a coupling defect — which is exactly why Gate B, which scores the coupling against the oracle flux map, does not report it as the dominant term.

Figure: moment-representation.svg / .png — three panels against clipped fraction (matched density extremes, centroid offset over circumradius, near-field error with zeroth-only overlaid), vertical rules at the sign-change fractions. Known cosmetic defect: the third panel's title is clipped at the right margin in the rendered PNG; the axis label and caption carry the meaning.
