Decisions re-read

  • Acceptance is convergence to the reference (lead 2026-09-10) — sound, and load-bearing for this review's verdict.
  • X-point cell now, not deferred (lead 2026-09-10) — sound.
  • The global tensor spline carries the read (lead 2026-09-10) — sound, and settled by Gate A: on the exact input the spline books the total to three parts in a hundred thousand and the ring quadratic matches it, while the linear edge root that production reads today falls 0.55 percent short with a median root error of 1.8 mm concentrated in the outboard cut cells. So the spline is not at fault and the ring quadratic would correct nothing. Note the tension that remains: §1.4 shows the placement's residual concentrates at the cell's own vertices, and a global spline fitted through those vertices will carry the band residual into every downstream flux-surface average.
  • Frozen blocks, clip enters through the moments (lead 2026-09-14) — sound, and it is what makes exact clipped blocks a refused candidate rather than an open one; order two beats the per-trip clipped blocks anyway (0.000115 of span against 0.000266) without touching the Biot geometry. Its own text asks "why the first-order route measured worse than zeroth order", and the record already answers it: order one is 0.044 to 0.054 of order zero on every row, so "that regression entered through the coupled state and support path, not through the coupling".
  • The census ring is the cell's vertex ring (lead 2026-09-14) — sound, and unmeasured. It is the right remedy for a measured failure: on the old neighbour-centroid ring the single-null saddle is missed at the first detector, the crossing histogram reading 0 once and 2 fifty-four times at 110 cells and the nearest ring at 0.71 pitch still reading two at 300, with a saddle found only at 500 where a ring sits 0.23 pitch away. Whether the vertex ring fixes it has not been run. The cost nobody costed stands: that ring reads the cut cell at the nodes where the matched density changes sign.

3. Verdict: the representation that keeps the frozen matrix without the artifact

A bounded-shift rule, paired with the order-two moments. Clamp the delivered first moment so the supported-centroid offset never exceeds 5R/24 = 0.2083 R.

Pair it with order two: the moment-order receipt adjudicates that only order two reaches 0.000266 of span (0.000133 upper envelope against order one's 0.000276). The clamp fixes where the residual sits; order two fixes how big it is. Neither substitutes for the other, and both rest on the moment-order number that finding 1 puts in dispute — which is why finding 1 is ranked first.

4. Second pass: §3, §6, §7 closed, and two of my own findings withdrawn

The evidence record's §5c and the frozen-blocks section were read after the first draft. They change three verdicts and retire two findings.

4.1 Two findings withdrawn

The "Gate B measures a gauge" finding is withdrawn as a finding and kept as an instrument note. The affine dominance is real and it is already in the record — §5c: "a best-fit function affine in R and Z explains 97 to 99 percent of its rms on every row, with a radial coefficient of 0.078 Wb per metre on the weak row, so it is a broad gradient across the domain and not a boundary-cell effect." The observation is the plan's, not mine. And the conclusion I drew from it is wrong: the record isolates the quantity the affine field hides, and the added first-order error is cut-cell localized even though the total is not — "first-order moments on interior cells alone give an rms over span of 0.0061 against the zeroth baseline of 0.0057, on cut cells alone 0.0101." My cut-over-interior ratio of 1.04–1.09 was computed on the total, which is gauge-dominated, so it could not have seen that delta.

The "41x disagreement" finding is withdrawn as unresolved-business. The record answers it: "order one is 0.044 to 0.054 of order zero on every row … that regression entered through the coupled state and support path, not through the coupling." Gate B ran on the certificate's production support, moment-order on the analytic-state exact clip. The sign hypothesis is settled negative too: the first-order-coupling run shows the kernel reproduces the exact ring-inductance flux to 2.2e-13 Wb rms on an interior hexagon (against 6.5e-4 zeroth-only and 1.3e-3 sign-flipped), and flipping "happens to mimic the misplaced dipole".

4.2 One finding independently confirmed

The plan's own moment-order run reports "the projection of a density confined to a sub-polygon onto a linear basis over the whole hexagon leaves a relative residual of 0.17 to 0.20 on cut cells against 5e-5 to 1.5e-4 on whole cells, and the quadratic basis halves the near-field figure rather than removing it." This review's own-vertex worst first-order rms relative error is 0.1694 — inside that band, from a different instrument (axisymmetric Green's function over the exact clipped polygon, on a cell rebuilt from the committed receipt). Two independent routes, one number. It also confirms that order two does not remove this residual, which is exactly the gap the bounded-shift rule fills.

4.4 What the ladder actually says, now that §7 of the record is read

Gate C's plateau is not convergence and not a globalisation — it is the outer loop's stopping rule: "a trip whose cut set and frozen coupling blocks are both unchanged re-solves the same problem to the same iterate, so the plateau is the loop's stopping rule reached." Newton-on-geometry beats Picard decisively on residual (weak 0.015→0.0016, moderate 0.008→0.0015) and no row gets closer to the reference: axis errors 53, 27, 8 mm against a committed 2–3 mm.

And a number that recurs and is not yet explained: the terminal amplitude shortfall and the boundary-level error are the same five percent on every limited row (weak boundary −2.20 Wb against an axis flux of 44.7). The record flags it as its own open question, separate from the coupling. It is the same order as the outboard hole (finding 1) and nothing has yet tested whether they are one thing.

Where the low-field-side current comes from

Node cca-which-mechanism-puts-current-on-the-low-field-side, run r-20260921T054108302938-..., worktree base 0322e911a52588e116bb76a2243ff0dc728cc655. Answer. The boundary band is not clipped at all. On the production route the current density is integrated over the whole atomic cell with no separatrix test, because the psi_norm > 1 clamp lives on a composite profile the moment path builds only when a scrape-off continuation is declared, and the Solov'ev certificate rows declare none. At the cold seed of weak-rotation-reactor-static, 73 of 135 cells with zero area inside the separatrix carry 44.5 % of the plasma current, 28 % more per cell on the low-field side because j ∝ R p'. §2b has the numbers, §5 the repair.

Section 1 is read from source and from exact polygon algebra; sections 2 to 4 are the instrumented production route, all_debug job 1274396, JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu, TMPDIR=/tmp, the root interpreter run directly with uv never invoked on the node, each row its own process of instrument_boundary_band.py, which wraps the committed route without editing it. §1b is a hypothesis this node carried in and its own measurement refuted; it is kept because the bound it establishes becomes load-bearing the moment the repair lands.

1. What the production route actually does (read from source, not argued)

_SUPPORT_CLIP_MODE defaults to "chord" (nova/equilibrium/forward_operator.py:105), and production never changes the mode. So on both named rows the support is:

chord_support = atomic_mesh.traced_clip(ones).qualify(masks.profile_participation)

(forward_operator.py:3041-3046). The clip is handed a uniform field of ones, so no cell is ever marked boundary; every selected cell is a whole atomic cell carrying its full polygon. On the production route there is no clipped polygon at all. Three of the fence's candidates are therefore refuted by construction on these two rows, and each would need set_support_clip_mode("exact") to be reachable:

candidateverdict on the production routeinstrument
clipped polygon carries the complement (vacuum side) on one traversal orientationrefuted — no clipped polygon is formed; traced_clip receives ones, boundary is all-falseforward_operator.py:3042-3046
sign flip in polygon orientation on one side of the traversalrefuted, same reason — the only polygons are the carrier's own atomic cells, authored oncesame
clipped coupling places the current as a filament at a pointrefuted — _quadrature_from_arrays (clip_quadrature.py:348-382) is a fixed-shape degree-15 Duffy rule over the fan triangulation of the polygon; the current is integrated over the cell, not sampled at a pointclip_quadrature.py:700-713
profile evaluated outside the separatrix and extrapolated beyond psi_norm 1NOT refuted — this is the mechanism; see §2b. An earlier draft of this table refuted it by citing the psi_norm > 1 clamp. That clamp belongs to _FluxSelectedProfile (source.py:86-105), which ForwardSource.current_moments constructs only when common_sol is declared (source.py:960-964). These rows declare none, so the quadrature integrates DomainProfile.current_density (source.py:369-373), which contains no separatrix test at allsource.py:86-105, source.py:960-964, source.py:369-373

The pin explains gate-c-resolve amplitude 31.04, not the artefactplan receipts

So the surviving candidates are the profile evaluation and the delivery of the first moment. §1b states the delivery hypothesis; §2 measures it and refutes it; §2b measures the profile evaluation and confirms it.

1b. The hypothesis the measurement tests: the first-order moment is delivered as a dipole

The route integrates, per cell, over the whole polygon with the density zeroed where psi_norm > 1 (§1). So for a cell the separatrix crosses it obtains a genuinely partial-cell pair — total current Q and first moment M about the cell centroid — both of them correct. It then calls ForwardFluxOperator.coupling_current_moments (forward_operator.py:2942-2973), which converts M into gradient coefficients by inverting the cell's second area moment tensor over the whole cell:

determinant = Ixx*Iyy - Ixy**2
radial   = ( Iyy*M_r - Ixy*M_z) / determinant
vertical = ( Ixx*M_z - Ixy*M_r) / determinant

That is the documented design — "the cut cells with a current gradient exist to shift the cell's effective centroid to the clipped region without editing the geometric interaction matrix" (lead, 2026-09-21). What the frozen matrix is then fed is not a current Q sitting at the supported region's centroid; it is the linear density over the entire cell whose zeroth and first moments are Q and M:

rho(x) = Q/A + (I^-1 M) . (x - x_c)      over the WHOLE cell

A linear function cannot represent a current confined to part of a cell. Beyond a bounded offset it necessarily goes negative on the far side of the cell, and the cell stops being a shifted monopole and becomes a monopole plus a strong dipole.

The bound, measured

For the hex carrier's regular cell of circumradius R the offset at which the delivered density first touches zero at the far vertex is

|M|/Q  =  Ixx / (A * R)  =  5R/24  =  0.2083 R

Measured (linear-moment-dipole-bound.txt, exact polygon moments of the regular hexagon, area=2.598076 R^2, Ixx=Iyy=0.541266 R^4):

supported-centroid offset `M/Q`delivered rho_max/rho_meandelivered rho_min/rho_meancell area carrying negative current
0.05 R1.240.760
0.2083 R2.000.000 (the bound)
0.30 R2.44−0.446.1 %
0.40 R2.92−0.9215.3 %
0.50 R3.40−1.4022.3 %
0.80 R4.84−2.8432.6 %
1.00 R5.80−3.8036.0 %

A cell whose supported sliver sits against one edge has an offset of order R, so the frozen matrix is handed a density running from +5.8 to −3.8 times the cell mean — a dipole roughly five times the monopole it was meant to shift. Every boundary cell in the band gets one. A chain of such dipoles along a band is exactly the signature the audit reports: "a chain of small closed ochre contours along the outboard boundary band", with the psi, gradient and hessian error panels confined to that band.

This is also consistent with the plan's own Gate B result, which the plan records independently of this node: the first-order term was measured making the frozen image worse on cut cells (rms over span 0.0057 → 0.0106), and the per-trip polygon-block line that removed the error was rejected as not the design.

Why the low-field side, as the hypothesis would have explained it

Superseded by the measured account in §2b; kept because item 1 survives that supersession and carries the outboard bias of the real mechanism too.

Three effects all act outboard and none acts inboard, and they multiply:

  1. The current density itself. The toroidal source is j = R p'(psi_n) + ff'(psi_n)/(mu0 R) (source.py:369-373). Its pressure term grows linearly with R, so an outboard boundary cell carries more current than an inboard one at the same psi_norm, and the same fractional error is a larger absolute dipole.
  2. The flux gradient across a cell. On the low-field side the separatrix runs through the widest part of the poloidal cross-section, so the supported fraction of a cell changes fastest per cell there — the population of small, far-offset slivers (the right-hand rows of the table) is an outboard population.
  3. The kernel weighting. The flux a ring source drives scales with its radius, so a dipole at large R writes a larger flux perturbation into the image than the same dipole at small R.

Item 1 is read off the source and is measured in §2b: the fully-outside cells carry 28 % more current per cell on the low-field side. Items 2 and 3 were reasoning and are now moot — they explain an outboard bias in the dipole, and §2 shows there is no dipole.

2. The measurement: the dipole hypothesis is refuted on the cold-seed row

all_debug job 1274396, JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu, TMPDIR=/tmp in the submit environment and the payload, the root interpreter run directly, uv never invoked on the node, each row its own process. Driver instrument_boundary_band.py; it wraps the committed route — every quantity comes from the operator's own _support_partition, support_current_moments and coupling_current_moments calls, and from the same degree-15 Duffy rule the moments used. Record: record/cold-seed-weak-rotation-reactor-static.json. Analysis: logs/cold-seed-analysis.txt.

Row cold-seed/weak-rotation-reactor-static, requested −110 cells, realised 135, target current 1.63148e7 A, analytic axis at R = 6.200 m.

tripresidualamplitudeunscaled Aownedlivebandband current Aband sharecells with negative delivered densitymax smax density swing
0 (cold seed)—0.896841.81915e7135135284.4266e60.243300.030.01
11.041e-150.896841.81915e7135135464.8166e60.264800.030.01
21.041e-150.896841.81915e7135135464.8166e60.264800.030.01

s is the admissibility measure of §1b: the supported-centroid offset divided by the offset Ixx/(A·R) at which the delivered linear density first touches zero. The hypothesis predicted s > 1 in the band. The measurement returns s ≤ 0.03, with a median of 0.02 and a delivered density running from 0.99 to 1.01 of the cell mean. Not one cell of 135, on any of the three states, carries a negative delivered density. The first-order moment on this row is a shift of about two percent of the admissible offset — a well-behaved centroid correction, not a dipole. §1b's algebra is correct; its premise fails for a reason §2b gives, and not for the reason it first appeared to. Thin slivers are present — the band's minimum inside fraction is 0.0139 at the seed and 0.0436 at the settled state, with 9 band cells below a quarter — so the far-offset regime is reached geometrically. The first moment simply does not follow the sliver. §2b measures s against the clipped fraction directly and shows why.

And the low-field side carries no excess at these states. Of 46 band cells, 21 are outboard of the analytic axis and 25 inboard:

sideband cellsband currentmax cell currentmax smedian sworst delivered density minnegative cells
low-field212.4956e6 A1.9234e5 A0.030.020.990
high-field252.3210e6 A1.5613e5 A0.030.020.990

A 7.5 % current difference across an 8 % cell-count difference, with the outboard cells carrying more because j = R p' + ff'/(μ₀R) grows with R (§1b). That is the expected Solov'ev behaviour, not an artefact.

One number is worth carrying to the re-review independently of the mechanism: the boundary band carries 26.5 % of the plasma current at 135 cells (4.82e6 A of 1.81915e7 A). The plan's working assumption that "cut cells carry only a few percent of the plasma current, so a factor of two cannot come from them" does not hold at this resolution. The factor-of-two amplitude discrepancy between the chord and exact supports is not excluded by a cut-cell current budget of this size.

2b. The mechanism, named with the numbers that prove it: the density is never clipped

The coordinator asked for the per-cell current against the clipped fraction. That plot is the proof, and it rules the dipole out and the profile evaluation in at the same time. Log: logs/s-versus-clipped-fraction.txt.

Cold seed, 28 band cells, binned by the fraction of the cell inside the separatrix:

inside fractioncellsmedian smax smedian cell currentband currentmedian centroid psi_norm
[0.00, 0.10)60.0280.0281.771e5 A1.014e6 A1.144
[0.25, 0.50)70.0250.0271.505e5 A1.106e6 A1.036
[0.50, 0.75)50.0250.0271.564e5 A7.919e5 A0.984
[0.75, 0.95)60.0280.0281.698e5 A7.765e5 A0.870
[0.95, 1.00)40.0270.0281.847e5 A7.387e5 A0.759

Read the two columns that matter.

s is flat. A cell 1.4 % inside the separatrix carries the same first-order shift as a cell 99.6 % inside: 0.028 against 0.027. corr(s, 1 − fraction) = +0.133 at the seed and −0.882 at the settled state, where the thinnest slivers have the smallest shift. The dipole hypothesis of §1b predicts the opposite and predicts s > 1; measured, s never exceeds 0.03 anywhere. Refuted against the variable it was a hypothesis about.

The current is flat too, and that is the defect. A cell 5 % inside carries 1.771e5 A; a cell 97 % inside carries 1.847e5 A. A twentyfold difference in supported area produces a 4 % difference in current. The current a cell contributes is very nearly independent of how much of that cell is inside the plasma.

Both follow from one line of source. ForwardSource.current_moments (source.py:960-964) selects

profile = self.core if self.common_sol is None else _FluxSelectedProfile(self.core, self.common_sol)

The psi_norm > 1 test — the thing that makes a clipped cell carry only its clipped current — lives on _FluxSelectedProfile.current_density (source.py:92-105). These rows declare no common_sol, so the quadrature is handed self.core, a bare DomainProfile whose current_density (source.py:369-373) is toroidal_current_density(R, p'(psi_n), ff'(psi_n)) with no separatrix test of any kind. For Solov'ev, p' and ff' are constants, so that density is nonzero over the entire carrier and varies only with R. The sole thing confining the plasma is the whole-cell mask profile_participation = ~(private_flux | excluded_material) (domain.py:125-128), which is true for essentially every cell.

That reading is confirmed executably rather than by grep, on the built operator (logs/common-sol-confirmation.txt):

case weak-rotation-reactor-static: operator.source is ForwardSource
   common_sol   = None
   private_flux = None
   profile handed to the quadrature by current_moments: RotatingDomainProfile
   separatrix test present on that profile: False

(The same log's last two lines attempt to detect the clamp by introspecting __code__.co_names; that check is miscoded — open_support is a local, so it lives in co_varnames — and both its answers should be ignored. The clamp's presence on _FluxSelectedProfile and its absence on the core profile are established by reading source.py:92-105 against source.py:369-373, and by the type name above.)

So the boundary band is not clipped at all: it is the whole cell, carrying the profile extrapolated past psi_norm 1. The measured consequence:

statecells whose centroid is outside the separatrix yet carry currentcurrent thereshare of unscaled total
cold seed85 of 1351.003e7 A55.1 %
settled (trip 1)20 of 1353.561e5 A2.0 %

The positive control, and the unambiguous number

A leak this large is a claim about the instrument first, so the check has to show the instrument resolving something known present. It does: inside_fraction separates the owned cells into three classes with the right normalised fluxes — fully inside (median psi_norm 0.398), straddling, and fully outside (median psi_norm 1.476). A zero would have been visible. Log: logs/leak-positive-control.txt.

Cold seed, 135 owned cells:

classcellscurrentmedian centroid psi_norm
fully inside the separatrix345.6664e6 A0.398
straddling284.4266e6 A—
fully outside — zero area inside the plasma738.0985e6 A1.476

Seventy-three cells with no part inside the separatrix carry 44.5 % of the unscaled plasma current, at 1.1e5 A each, the smallest of them still carrying 497 A. That is not a clipping error of degree; there is no clip. And it is outboard-biased in exactly the way j ∝ R p' predicts: the 28 low-field fully-outside cells carry 1.280e5 A each against 1.003e5 A for the 45 high-field ones, 28 % more per cell.

One trip later only 8 cells remain fully outside, carrying 0.2 %. The solve has not fixed the leak — it has moved the boundary out to swallow it, which is why the trip-1 panel shows the band hugging the vessel (§3) and why the gate-c row terminates with its axis against the outboard wall at amplitude 31. An unclipped current outside the separatrix is a current the fixed point can only reconcile by enlarging the plasma.

Why the low-field side and not the high-field side — measured

j = R p' + ff'/(μ₀R) grows with R, so the unclipped current that leaks across the separatrix is largest at large major radius. At the cold seed the leak splits:

sideleaking cellsleaked currentper cell
low-field324.308e6 A1.346e5 A
high-field535.722e6 A1.080e5 A

25 % more leaked current per cell on the outboard side, and the outboard leak sits in the open region against the vessel where no confined current balances it. That is the gate-c receipt's description exactly — "one lump of current at the outboard boundary and its nested contours close on an axis sitting against the outboard wall" — and, at the low amplitudes of the cold-seed row, a weaker version of the same thing distributed along the outboard band, which is the island chain.

What this mechanism does NOT yet explain, stated plainly

The settled state leaks only 2.0 %, and its panel shows no island chain (§3). So the leak is large at the seed and small one trip later, and this node did not reach the four-trip terminal state where the audit figures show the artefact (§4). The mechanism is established as present and outboard-biased; that it is the cause of the terminal island chain is inference, not measurement, until the leak is measured at that state.

2c. Row 2, gate-c-resolve/diverted-single-null at 1074 cells — partial

The second row ran in the same allocation and did not finish inside the fence. Its driver writes the per-cell record only at process exit, so what survives is the per-trip line the driver prints as each trip lands, in logs/band-record.log:

tripresidualexecutedamplitudeprofile-owned cellsband cellswall s
0 (cold seed)——0.610093—10431.8
10.99062810.603486107058237.7
20.11976520.618121104599712.3

Two things are readable from this much and no more. The residual falls by an order of magnitude between trips 1 and 2, so unlike row 1 this row does not settle after one trip and its later trips are reachable — the four-trip trajectory §4 says row 1 failed to reproduce is available here. And the amplitude stays near 0.61 across all three trips, nowhere near the committed row's terminal 31.04, which is consistent with §2b's account: the amplitude inflation is a late consequence of a support the leak has driven outward, not a property of the early trips.

The row then died, and the log names the cause. At 2026-09-21T08:20:02+02:00, about six minutes into trip 3's compilation:

E0921 08:20:01.932947 execution_engine.cc:54] LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory
E0921 08:20:01.933085 execution_engine.cc:54] LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory
E0921 08:20:01.933228 execution_engine.cc:54] LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory
slurm_script: line 18: 927566 Segmentation fault (core dumped)
ROW2_EXIT=139

The trip-3 program at 1074 cells exhausted the job's 64 GB during LLVM compilation, not during execution, and the backend then segfaulted rather than raising. No per-cell record and no panel exists for this row, and none will without a resubmission. Nothing in §2b rests on it.

This is a finding the next node must carry, and it is the same failure the plan already records for four drivers across three plans — "the driver aborts in compilation … the process at 65,271 memory mappings against a limit of 65,530." Here the mechanism is explicit: reduced_newton.py:1369 unrolls the active-set loop in Python, so program size grows with the trip cap, and at 1074 cells the cap-3 program does not compile in 64 GB. Walking trips by capping the cap is therefore not merely expensive, it does not reach the later trips at production resolution at all. Emitting the per-trip state from inside that loop is one compile of the size that already works, and it is the only route to the four-trip terminal state §4 says this node failed to reach.

3. The panels, and what they show

Rendered with the committed nova.media painters under the plotting rules, PNG and SVG:

  • panels/cold-seed-weak-rotation-reactor-static-trip-0.{png,svg}
  • panels/cold-seed-weak-rotation-reactor-static-trip-1.{png,svg}
  • panels/cold-seed-weak-rotation-reactor-static-trip-2.{png,svg} (the same state as trip 1; the solve settles at trip 1, see §4)

I opened trip 0 and trip 1 with the image read tool.

Trip 0 (cold seed). Analytic blue and solved ochre contours are both nested and closed about a common axis, the two axis markers nearly coincident near the geometric centre. Every one of the 135 cells carries a red (positive) glyph, and the glyph sizes vary little from the axis to the wall, growing modestly toward the outboard side. The outlined band cells form an interior annulus at roughly mid-radius, not a rim: at the seed the cell-local psi_norm = 1 surface cuts through the middle of the mesh. There is no closed ochre contour anywhere outside the main nest, and no blue glyph — no cell carries negative current.

Trip 1 (settled). The band outlines have moved to the outermost ring of cells, hugging the vessel: 46 cells around the full circumference, inboard and outboard alike. The contours are smooth, nested and close, the solved ochre lying systematically just outside the analytic blue near the edge. Glyphs remain uniformly positive, uniformly sized, and present right out to the wall — which is correct for Solov'ev, whose p' and ff' are constant so the current density does not vanish at the separatrix. There is no island chain and no closed outboard contour on this panel.

That last sentence is the important one, and it is a negative result about the state, not about the method: the states this node measured are not the states the audit figures show.

One caveat on the panels' own instrument. Row 1's record carries no solved null set: the driver read topology.magnetic_axis, and the attribute is topology.axis (nova/equilibrium/topology.py:61), so the lookup returned nothing. The panels fall back to the extremum of their own raster and say so in the caption. On trip 0 two axis markers are separately visible and nearly coincident; on trip 1 only one is discernible, and this node did not separate "coincident with the analytic marker" from "dropped by the wall-containment test". The driver was corrected mid-run, so row 2's record carries the route's own read nulls; row 1's panels should be re-rendered from a corrected record before they are cited as evidence about the solved axis. Everything else on the panels — contours, wall, band outlines, glyphs — comes from the record and is unaffected.

4. Why these states are not the artefact state, stated as an instrument fault

The committed receipt for this row reports four trips and a terminal fixed-point residual of 7.4e-3, not converged, with the island chain. My capped solves report executed = 1 for every requested cap — 1, 2 and 5 alike — and equilibrium.fixed_point.residual of 1.041e-15. Thirteen orders of magnitude between the two residuals on nominally the same row is not a small disagreement, and the honest reading is that one of two things is wrong and I have not separated them: either equilibrium.fixed_point.residual is not the quantity the receipt calls terminal_fixed_point_residual (it may be the last inner Newton residual on a frozen active set, which would be machine-small by construction), or the active_set_steps override in my request changed the exit rule and the solve settles where the committed configuration keeps tripping. Either way the artefact state was not reproduced, so the per-cell record above describes the seed and a one-trip state, and the mechanism behind the island chain remains unmeasured.

This also means the four refutations in §1 and the refutation of §1b in §2 stand on different footings. §1 is structural: it is true of the route on any state. §2's s ≤ 0.03 is a statement about two particular states, and a re-dispatch that reaches the four-trip terminal state must re-measure s there before the dipole is dismissed.

5. The repair the mechanism implies — ready to append as a followup

> Give the core profile a separatrix test, or stop calling the band clipped. > ForwardSource.current_moments (nova/equilibrium/source.py:960-964) wraps the core > profile in _FluxSelectedProfile — the only thing that zeroes current density above > psi_norm 1 (source.py:92-105) — only when common_sol is declared. The > Solov'ev certificate rows declare none, so the quadrature integrates > DomainProfile.current_density (source.py:369-373), which has no separatrix test, > over the whole atomic cell; confinement rests entirely on the whole-cell mask > profile_participation = ~(private_flux | excluded_material) > (nova/equilibrium/domain.py:125-128). Measured on the production route at 135 cells: > the per-cell current is independent of the clipped fraction to within 4 % across a > twentyfold range of supported area, s never exceeds 0.03 and is anticorrelated > with the sliver thinness (−0.882 at the settled state), 85 of 135 cells whose centroid > lies outside the separatrix carry current, and that leaked current is 55.1 % of the > unscaled total at the cold seed, biased outboard at 1.346e5 A per cell against > 1.080e5 A inboard because j = R p' + ff'/(μ₀R) grows with R. > Repair: apply the psi_norm > 1 test unconditionally on the moment path — the > zero-continuation case is _FluxSelectedProfile with an absent open profile, which > source.py:100-101 already handles — so a cell straddling the separatrix integrates > only its confined part whether or not a scrape-off continuation is declared. Gate it > on the certificate rows with the leaked-current fraction above as the measure: it must > fall to zero, and the amplitude must move toward unity. Re-measure s afterwards, > because a correctly clipped cell is exactly the case §1b bounds, and the dipole this > node refuted becomes reachable the moment the clip starts working.

The two guards this node also earned

  • Carry s = (|M|/Q) / (Ixx/(A·R)) as a per-row receipt field with max(s) > 1 as a refusal. It is cheap, and after the repair above it is the bound that decides whether the first-order shift is a centroid correction or a dipole (§1b: at s = 4.8 the delivered density runs +5.8 to −3.8 of the cell mean over 36 % of the cell).
  • Correct the plan's working assumption. The boundary band carries 26.5 % of the plasma current at 135 cells, not a few percent, so a cut-cell current budget does not exclude the factor-of-two amplitude discrepancy between the chord and exact supports.

The next node's first task is still the instrument

Settle the residual disagreement of §4 before attributing the terminal island chain: run the row through benchmarks.solovev_certificate._measure unmodified, confirm four trips and a terminal residual near 7.4e-3, and measure the leaked-current fraction there. Two mechanics found this session make that cheaper:

  • reduced_newton.py:1369 iterates trips in a Python for loop, so every distinct active_set_steps is its own compilation (194 s for cap 1, 559 s cumulative for cap 2 at 135 cells). Emitting the per-trip state from inside that loop is one compile.
  • masks.profile_participation is the domain the profile owns, not the current-carrying set, so 135 of 135 is neither a collapsed nor an inflated support.

Separatrix test repair review

Verdict: promote with corrected criteria. Score: 88/100.

Repair reviewed: a493a1f4f11102ee20a4af6e7869a42cbbc27b4d on base 7c97f916d81c041fda079d82ee963f7788863e20. The commit changes ForwardSource.current_moments to hand _FluxSelectedProfile(self.core, self.common_sol) to quadrature unconditionally and adds tests/test_moment_path_separatrix_test.py; no other production path changes. _FluxSelectedProfile.current_density returns exactly zero for psi_norm > 1 when no common-SOL continuation exists and preserves the core density at and below the separatrix.

Reproduction and negative control

The defect reproduces against the base source with the repair's test: test_moment_path_applies_the_separatrix_test_at_the_seed fails because the captured quadrature profile is a bare DomainProfile, not _FluxSelectedProfile. The source-level negative control, MUTATION: restoring the bare self.core selection, fails the same assertion. The repaired source passes the case. This is the required refusal rather than an inference from a passing suite.

Boundary-band receipt

The original instrument and analyser were loaded from the diagnostic report with SHA-256 08246c10719315b085e1176ee2502bd4b420e9d41ab07e28539c4286a364370e and 1be3206af2ed392584801d30ff11f72ba2cb050165e9d6fa0bdb982eba3d6740. One all_debug allocation, job 1274492, used JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu, TMPDIR=/tmp in submit and payload, the shared root interpreter directly, and the repair worktree on PYTHONPATH.

The cold-seed receipt is complete and gives:

MeasureBeforeRepair
outside-centroid cells visible to the instrument8585
outside-centroid cells carrying current8512
current in outside-centroid cells / unscaled total55.1%4.9144724741%
unscaled currentnot retained by this review8.00491 MA
target current16.3148 MA16.3148 MA
seed amplitude0.89682.0380954023
arithmetic check, target / unscaled—2.0380954023

The instrument sees the known-present set of 85 outside-centroid cells, so the reduction is not an empty-census artifact. The twelve survivors are cut cells whose quadrature has confined-side support; counting their legitimate confined current as leakage makes the centroid metric invalid after pointwise clipping. The direct guarded quantity is instead the density evaluated at psi_norm > 1, and the repaired test pins it to exactly zero.

The amplitude clause is arithmetically reversed. Under pinned plasma current, amplitude is target / unscaled. Removing extrapolated current lowers the unscaled total, so amplitude must rise, here from the recorded 0.8968 to 2.0380954023; movement toward unity is not an acceptance property of this repair. A later allocation/candidacy repair may change the confined unscaled total, but this commit cannot simultaneously remove the former current and preserve the earlier amplitude.

The max-five-trip process got beyond the earlier 64 GiB failure and printed trip 1 (residual 5.72675e-2, amplitude 2.02831) and trip 2 (same residual and amplitude). It later aborted in LLVM allocation after an overlapping 16 GiB test step was started in the same allocation, so no max-five-trip JSON or terminal residual is claimed. The durable seed receipt was written first in a sibling step of the same allocation. This instrumentation failure costs twelve review points and must not be read as a physics failure.

Corrected promotion criteria

Promote a493a1f4f with the following correction to the recorded gate:

  1. The pointwise no-continuation profile must return exactly zero above psi_norm = 1 and preserve the core density inside; the base and bare-core negative controls must fail this assertion.
  2. The centroid census is diagnostic only: report total outside-centroid cells, live outside-centroid cells and their confined-current share. Do not require the share to be below one percent or zero, because a clipped cell may have its centroid outside while retaining a real confined polygon.
  3. Under a pinned target current, require the reported amplitude to equal target_current / unscaled_current; do not require movement toward unity from this repair alone.
  4. Keep the standing forward-axes failure as a follow-on outside this node. A merged-head verification node owns the combined result.

No source under nova/ was edited or committed by this review.

Negative-control audit

The three joined ledger rows contain no negative_control or negative_control_check value. Therefore the count of ledger-recorded negative controls to compare is zero. This is an evidence limitation, not a claim that no worker ran a red arm.

The third worker manifest cites gate1.log as a red intermediate criterion. It is real: 32 passed and two diverted-contact tests failed because the any reachable candidate criterion moved the contact by 0.0248156 m across refinements and 0.159439 m from the analytic extremum. It is not ledger-linked, and its first line does not repeat a declared mutation. It therefore supports the final design choice but does not satisfy a ledger negative-control contract.

The first two promotion manifests contain no negative-control log. Neither assertion flipping, a stub gate, a widened tolerance, a self-disabling guard, nor captioning around a physics claim was found in their scoped diffs. The first promotion's false-green ledger classification is the process finding instead.

Two unpromoted local commits

74f00821cf6b1b0e5c649022b5eca55f6d2d3f31

Verdict: the tracer repair is technically sound in isolation, but the commit is not sound to promote as completion of its node.

44ecd9443cbd106d4acef14ba8fe1921d69f759b

Verdict: defective; do not promote.

Its negative control is correctly constructed: base-revision connectivity_boundary.py with the fixture assertion unmodified fails inf != -0.31887492472951007, exit 1. The candidate makes that grid gate green by recovering a whole-wall level when the supplied contact has no reachable support. It simultaneously adds a failure to test_diverted_mast_row_polishes_only_its_census_wall_candidate; the independent separation probe confirms the candidate bit can remain false while class_u_wall becomes finite, but the MAST pin explicitly requires that level to remain infinite. This is a semantic contradiction, not a tolerance issue. The lead's ruling that both reachability verdicts are discretisation artifacts means the next repair must change the contact/reachability mechanism, not pick one existing pin and weaken the other.

Convergence-atlas mechanism

The 22086/43 mixed panel is converged (terminal_residual = 1.7364758673167596e-14) and its receipt marks an admitted saddle at (0.56481685, 1.25802216) m. The stored red curve closes at Z [-0.73576, 0.79994] m and does not pass through that saddle.

The numeric probe in atlas-level-probe.txt first proves the instrument sees known-present, distinct values: the saddle, upper wall point, lower wall candidate, and all 487 boundary points evaluate finite. The boundary points form one near-isoflux set, mean 0.0177760953 Wb with standard deviation 1.858e-05 Wb. The saddle is -0.1220793233 Wb, 0.139855 Wb away; the upper and lower wall candidates are 0.0195717508 and 0.0165463691 Wb, only 0.001796 and 0.001230 Wb away. The curve is therefore a wall-contact-level boundary, not the admitted-saddle separatrix.

The rule that produced it is in the core path, not the painter:

  1. generate_topology_visuals.py:269 calls operator.read(state) without a requested class.
  2. At the renderer base, the class-free topology read lets Topology.boundary compare the saddle and wall contact; when the wall wins, boundary_flux = data_w[2].
  3. generate_topology_visuals.py:364,375 uses that topology.boundary_flux for the atomic signed flux and assemble_separatrix_branches, persisting the resulting curve as nova_boundary.
  4. poloidal_convergence_atlas.py:490 then labels any nonempty nova_boundary as diverted; it never reads the topology class or checks that the level equals the saddle flux.

Thus the panel faithfully draws the core method's wall-level output and misstates its class. Per the lead's 2026-09-21 ruling, this is a mechanism defect with a diagnostic figure, not a painter defect. The repair is to carry and enforce the governed class and boundary level, then regenerate the panel.

Followups

findings.json contains four complete edit_plan append items: false-green gate integrity on figure-and-solver-audit; atlas regeneration, completion/splitting of 74f00821c, and replacement of 44ecd9443 under null-identification-authority. Each body names the commit, file, line, defect, and repair.

cut-cell-current-attribution — 114 figures, all opened, mean 67.4, 52 under 70, 11 misrepresenting physics

114 figures (77 PNG, 37 SVG) across 33 sub-directories beside 454 JSON receipts. All 114 were opened with the image read tool and scored; the 37 SVGs were rendered with rsvg-convert and opened as images, so the reviewer saw what a reader sees. Mean 67.4 / 100. 52 of 114 (46%) score under 70. Eleven misrepresent their physics. 40 of the 114 are cited by no document in docs/; 17 of the 37 SVGs carry full matplotlib axis furniture; three sub-directories carry figures and zero receipts.

The eleven physics misrepresentations

  1. gate-c-resolve/diverted-single-null-production-route-cells-1000.png (38) and …-cells-500.png (38) — both converged False; the solved field closes on an axis against the outboard wall, outside the analytic separatrix, with no solved X-point marked at all. Neither has a separatrix or an admitted saddle under a diverted-single-null caption.
  2. wholecell-rows/…/weak-…-cells-2500.png (40) — residual=0.000e+00 · converged=yes beside its own psi-error panel running to 3.17e+01 Wb. An exact fixed point cannot carry a 31.7 Wb error; the residual reported is not the residual of the state drawn.
  3. wholecell-rows/…/diverted-…-cells-1000.png (45) — solved axis outside its own separatrix while the saddle is found.
  4. rung-a-certificate/diverted-single-null-production-route-reduced.png (40) — residual 6.354e-01, and the solved X-point drawn at the inboard midplane.
  5. gate-b/absolute-flux-error.svg (45) — panels quoting max |error| 0.9752 Wb rendered blank, because the levels sit above the stated maximum.
  6. cut-cell-coupling/weak-110-coupling-errors.svg (50) — a rms 0.00000 panel, empty, in the middle of a before/after sequence.
  7. positional-stiffness/…-cells-110-residual.png and …-cells-300-residual.png (45 each) — pixel-identical under two different cell counts.
  8. limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow.png (30) — no text of any kind, and the draw_nulls axis triangle placed in the divertor leg.
  9. zero-residual/panels/zero-residual-map.png (48) — a panel titled “terminal flux (persisted)” that draws no flux.

Cross-cutting

  • The shared solve-panel template obeys axes, unfilled contours, stated levels and the wall, and omits half its null vocabulary: no nulls on any error panel, one null set only on several comparison panels, hollow qualified-null markers absent directory-wide.
  • 25 figures across five templates print no residual and no converged flag, while five sibling templates print both from the same renderer.
  • Three newton-geometry figures are their chord_live siblings with the residual and trip count stripped from the title; two more figures exist as duplicate PNG/SVG pairs.
  • All fourteen gate-c-resolve figures are unqualified · converged False.
  • exact-clip-memory/base-memory-scaling.svg is hand-authored SVG, not a nova.media render, with nothing binding its marks to a receipt.
  • Positive control, and the repair target: limited-shadow/lfs-contours-before-after.png (92) prints residual and converged on all nine subtitles and names in its footnote the row it does not draw and why.

Nineteen repair followups are staged for the owning plan cut-cell-current-attribution in findings-cut-cell-current-attribution.json.

Repair — the twelve exact-clip-seed panels state their terminal state and draw both null sets

The twelve panels scored 66 each above; F7 named them as printing no residual and no converged flag, and F3 as missing half the null vocabulary. All twelve were rebuilt from the committed parts/*.json receipts with no solve, and each title now states residual=… · converged=… read from that row's persisted solver block: 12 of 12, residuals 1.20e-13–6.71e-13 (1.1998637468319157e-13 low, 6.711667497659967e-13 high), every row converged True and class: limited with one magnetic axis and no admitted saddle. Every poloidal panel, the three error panels of the 2×2 production-route template included, now draws both the analytic and the solved null set in the draw_nulls vocabulary — solid triangle for the axis, filled cross for the admitted saddle, hollow markers for other qualified nulls — in the two contour colours (#3366cc analytic, #cc7722 solved), and an in-panel caption names which colour is which.

The reading is a render receipt (render-receipt.json) written by the render-only entry point and read back by a check in tests/test_exact_clip_seed_render.py: 12 figures, each title's residual and converged flag equal both to its part receipt and to the seed receipt's row, each figure's digest equal to the file on disk, and per poloidal panel both null sets recorded with all three glyph kinds. The receipt's own counts are one axis and no saddle per set for every row, so the vocabulary is exercised by its own cases in the same file: a positive control that tells the three glyph kinds apart, and a containment case that drops a saddle outside the vessel. The declared negative control strips residual= from one title in the receipt and the check is observed to refuse it, naming that figure.

Repair — docs/figures/cut-cell-current-attribution/limited-shadow/solve-panels/chord

Reviewer anchor: #figure-audit-cut-cell-current-attribution. The four chord panels scored 52, 60, 60 and 60 with the reviewer naming three faults: the title carried no terminal residual or converged flag, the diverted-single-null absolute-psi-error panel contoured machine round-off as a structured map, and the poloidal panels drew one null set where a second is handed to the painter.

What the repair is. A render-only entry point in benchmarks/limited_row_shadow_census.py (--render-only) rebuilds the four panels from the committed solve-parts/chord/*.json receipts — no forward operator, no solve — and writes a per-figure receipt. Each title now prints the terminal residual and the converged flag read from the receipt's own solver.terminal_fixed_point_residual and solver.converged; each absolute-error panel contours only levels at or above ROUNDOFF_LEVEL_FLOOR 1e-12 and its caption names the floor; every poloidal panel draws the analytic and the solved null set in their own colours, and the receipt records the glyph count each set drew.

Receipt. docs/figures/cut-cell-current-attribution/limited-shadow/render-receipt.json (schema nova.limited-shadow-render-receipt, four rows). Titles, as published: diverted-single-null · cells-500 · qualified · residual=2.423e-17 · converged=yes; moderate-rotation-conventional-static · cells-1000 · qualified · residual=5.875e-16 · converged=yes; strong-rotation-compact-static · cells-1000 · qualified · residual=2.913e-16 · converged=yes; weak-rotation-reactor-static · cells-1000 · qualified · residual=1.388e-15 · converged=yes. The control's psi panel is the round-off case: max_absolute 8.153e-17, levels empty, levels_suppressed_below_floor 8, caption round-off floor 1e-12 · levels: none (every contour level is below the floor) / this panel is round-off, not signal. Every one of the sixteen rendered panels reports at least one glyph per null set (the control 2 analytic / 2 solved; the three limited rows 1 / 1).

Gate. python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_limited_row_shadow_render.py at the node's base revision: 4 passed in 13.64 s, log render-receipt-check.log. The check reads the committed receipt, compares each title's residual= against the part receipt's own field, requires no contour level below the floor on any error panel and the caption to name it, requires both null sets on every panel, and additionally rebuilds the control panel in memory and reads the axes.

Negative control. Declared mutation sub-floor-level — restore the diverted psi error panel's unfiltered contour levels, so a level below the 1e-12 floor is contoured as signal. Applied, the check reddens with diverted-single-null/psi: contour levels below the round-off floor 1e-12: [4.72e-20], 1 failed, 3 passed, log render-receipt-check-negative-control.log.

Figures regenerated under the plotting rules: the four solve-panels/chord/*.png, on the login node only, from the persisted receipts alone (render 2.1 s, import 13.1 s). No SVG companion existed for these four before this repair and none was created.

repair — the empty terminal-flux panel of zero-residual/panels/zero-residual-map.png

Scored 48, misrepresenting physics: the moderate-rotation-conventional-static panel titled terminal flux (persisted) drew no field at all — only the boundary ring and a faceless axis dot — so the figure invited the reading that no persisted flux raster existed for that row. Reviewer anchor: docs/evidence/archive/figure-and-solver-audit-landed.html#figure-audit-cut-cell-current-attribution.

What the persisted raster turned out to be

The raster was present and finite for both rows. The measured span is -1.3328 Wb for moderate-rotation-conventional-static and -47.5943 Wb for weak-rotation-reactor-static, a factor of about 36 apart. The driver resolved one level array over the concatenation of both rasters, so all ten levels landed in the weak row's range; the narrow row contains none of them, and draw_flux_contours drew an empty level set. The defect is a level-array scope defect, not an absent or flat raster.

The repair

  • Levels are resolved per raster, so each panel states its own levels= count and is drawn on those levels for the nulls of its own row and the contours of its own raster.
  • Each terminal-flux panel records the finite range of the raster it drew on (raster_min_wb, raster_max_wb) and the number of its stated levels that strictly cross that range (levels_inside_raster). The moderate panel now states 10 levels, 8 of which lie inside [-0.3738, 1.1582] Wb; the weak panel states 10 levels, 8 inside [-12.5714, 41.6496] Wb.
  • Both null sets are drawn and counted. The solved terminal topology is drawn solid through the draw_nulls vocabulary and the analytic reference topology is drawn hollow in a distinct colour, so a solved null on a non-converged state no longer reads as the answer on its own. Both rows are limited with x_candidate_count = 0, so each set is a single magnetic axis: the receipt records solved_axis = 1 and reference_axis = 1 per panel.
  • Every panel title carries residual= and converged= on its own line.
  • render-receipt.json beside the panels, so the arithmetic above is checkable without opening the PNG.
  • A --render-only entry point rebuilds the panels from persisted render bundles without applying the map: 20.8 s wall on the login node, no solve, no device.

The gate, its negative control, and what it does not cover

tests/test_zero_residual_render.py asserts against the receipt that every panel title carries residual= and converged=, that each terminal-flux panel states as many levels as it lists, that the stated levels cross its own recorded raster range, and that both null sets contributed a glyph. Declared negative control: the moderate panel's level array is replaced with the union-derived array (the historical defect). Applied to the check, the check goes red with stated levels do not match the counted levels inside the raster (0 vs 8).

Two findings this repair does not close, both for the reviewer rather than the repair node. First, the shadow census is now empty for both rows: entering_cold equals the whole state (9123 of 9123 carriers for moderate-rotation-conventional-static, 9009 of 9009 for weak-rotation-reactor-static), where the reviewed revision recorded 73 and 54 excluded carriers. The measurement driver had no branch for an empty shadow census and aborted on np.max of an empty selection, overwriting its own acceptance receipt with an incomplete stub; the fix folds the empty selection to null. The shift is in the operator's shadow mask or its clip, not in the operator's carrier set. Second, the unmasked supremum moved with it, from 1.1236 to 1.1000 Wb for the moderate row and from 44.11 to 42.25 Wb for the weak row, so the residual story in the repaired figure is not the story the audit scored. With no shadowed carrier the relative-residual raster is concentrated in a thin shell at the boundary, so the right-hand panels of the repaired figure carry ink only there: their eight levels do cross their own raster and the panel is not the empty-array defect the left panel was, but a reader comparing the two figures would see the residual panel lose its interior structure. The gate below is the node's own change and reads the receipt; verifying the merged result belongs to a separately dispatched test node.

Positional-stiffness residual figure pair — two measurements, not one picture

Question asked. The repair followups on cut-cell-current-attribution reported that the 40-cell and 300-cell residual figures were pixel-identical and that translated-40mm.png printed neither its residual nor a convergence flag. The followups' own instruction was to find out why the pair looked identical — same receipt read twice, or identical receipts — and to say which.

Answer. Neither. Both figures are drawn from the one committed measurement receipt docs/.../positional-stiffness/receipt.json (sha256 6e0b30e9b875ceb1ee4714d1aee8476a8ff50ba71d476f3a34828783dec223bb), and each draws its own disjoint row selection out of it. They are not the same picture and never were; they are merely indistinguishable to a reader at the size they are printed, their arrays differing by under three percent at 40 mm. The pixel-identity claim is refuted by measurement, not by argument: the decoded-pixel digests differ, and so do the file digests.

figurerequested cellsrealised cellsPNG sha256 (first 16)pixel sha256 (first 16)
weak-rotation-reactor-static-cells-110-residual.png11013553b3208154062b6926c8e8428281fc31
weak-rotation-reactor-static-cells-300-residual.png30034246c3c9281231b6c754a5435489fd15f3
moderate-rotation-conventional-static-cells-300-residual.png300341fc9640a50ff30ba9c25d94d9e6c425a4

The comparison panel now states what it is. Each of the six panels of translated-40mm.png carries four title lines: the case and realised cell count, the displacement direction, the level array both fields are contoured on, and the residual is printed against the analytic span with a convergence flag.

The level array is stated. The two fields on a panel share one physical level array rather than one each, which is what makes a mismatch on the panel readable. The 110-cell panel states levels Wb (shared): [-19.51, -13.22, -6.931, -0.64, 5.651, 11.94, 18.23, 24.53, 30.82, 37.11, 43.4, 49.69].

Convergence reads no on all six panels, and that is the honest reading. The criterion is that the production map, started from the drawn 40 mm state, returns the magnetic axis inside the input displacement within four trips. At 40 mm it does not: the axis projects 0.0738 m (weak, outboard), 0.1391 m (weak, vertical), 0.0715 m (300-cell, outboard), 0.1370 m (300-cell, vertical), 0.0681 m (moderate, outboard) and 0.1142 m (moderate, vertical), every one above the 0.040 m input displacement. The flag is computed, not looked up, and at this amplitude it carries no discriminating power between the cases.

Both null sets are drawn and counted on every panel. The render receipt records the glyph count drawn per null set per panel. This fixture reads limited, so both sets draw their magnetic axis and no saddle: x_points_drawn is 0 for the analytic set and 0 for the translated set on all six panels, while axis_drawn is 1 for each. The saddle part of the vocabulary is therefore unexercised at this directory; the axis count is what a reader can hold this record to.

Instrument. Drawing is payload-driven: the driver rebuilds every named figure from the committed receipts with no operator, no production map and no four-trip iteration, so the whole render path runs on the login node in seconds (--render-only, and --write-render-inputs for the translated input). A render receipt render-receipt.json in the figure directory records, per figure, its title lines and its file and pixel digests, and per poloidal panel the glyph count drawn for each null set.

The guard. tests/test_fixture_positional_stiffness_render.py reads the receipt and the PNGs directly, with only the standard library and Pillow, so it does not rest on the code path that wrote them. Five checks pass against the shipped receipt (render/render-test.log). The declared negative control writes the alleged defect into the receipt — one figure's pixel digest copied onto the figure it is compared against — and the check reddens on it with the message the guard exists to emit (render/render-test-negative-control.log).

What remains. The pair are distinct measurements, but at the size they are printed they are still visually alike, so a reader cannot tell them apart by eye; only the digests and the row counts separate them. At 40 mm the iteration does not return inside its input displacement, so the convergence flag reads no everywhere at that amplitude, and the saddle vocabulary is unexercised because this fixture is limited in both topologies. Verifying the merged result belongs to a separately dispatched test node.

Commit c88ef0b91 on this worktree's branch.

Repair — limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow.png (30)

Repaired 2026-09-22 by node fsa-repair-contact-shadow-figure under followup f-cca-repair-contact-shadow-unlabelled, as a painter-only repair: both limiter-read figures are rebuilt from the committed receipt by the driver's render entry point with no solve entered. The defect reproduced before the repair. git show HEAD:docs/figures/cut-cell-current-attribution/limiter-read/single-null-contact-shadow.png is 972×1116 and carries no legend column and no title, and render-receipt.json was absent at HEAD, so no receipt described the committed artifact at all. The regenerated panel is 1404×1116.

What the regenerated figures report. render-receipt.json in the figure directory lists, per figure, every title line drawn and, per poloidal panel, the glyph count of every null set. For this panel: analytic_magnetic_axis 1 drawn, analytic_admitted_x_points 1 drawn, wall_contact_glyphs 1, excluded_* 18 exempted private-wall nodes, wall_drawn true at wall_node_count 481 (the source row's own wall array length).

Provenance of the two title fields the source receipt does not carry per row. The audit's reported residual and convergence flag have no per-row field: the source receipt enters no nonlinear solve (nonlinear_solve_entered false) because the field is analytic and only the carrier wall sampling varies, so the title's residual is the selected contact's production_contact.level_error_in_span against the exact wall extremum (9.852875804959903e-05) and converged reads n/a. The nominal cell scale and the 1074 realised cells are both stated in the title's second line.

The check that could have failed. tests/test_limiter_read_render.py asserts each of those conditions against the render receipt, selected through NOVA_LIMITER_READ_RENDER_RECEIPT so a mutated receipt can be audited. Under that control — the wall-contact marker reset to the axis triangle ^, its legend entry deleted and the title truncated to diverted-single-null · cells=1074 — the run fails on exactly those three conditions and the verbatim mutation is line 1 of the recorded log.

The convergence-atlas boundary is a core-method wall level

The converged 22086/43 mixed receipt reports residual 1.7364758673167596e-14 and an admitted saddle at (0.56481685, 1.25802216) m. A cubic-spline probe over its committed 33 by 33 field sees all operands as finite and distinct: saddle flux -0.1220793233 Wb, published upper wall point 0.0195717508 Wb and lower wall candidate 0.0165463691 Wb. The 487 stored boundary points form a near-isoflux curve at mean 0.0177760953 Wb with standard deviation 1.858e-05 Wb and close inside Z [-0.73576, 0.79994] m. The curve is 0.139855 Wb from the saddle but only 0.001230 Wb from the lower wall candidate. The instrument therefore sees a known-present saddle and wall level; the wall-level verdict is not inferred from an empty result.

The producing rule is the defect. generate_topology_visuals.py:269 calls operator.read(state) without a requested class. The class-free topology path lets Topology.boundary compare the saddle and wall contact; when the wall wins it publishes data_w[2]. The producer then traces topology.boundary_flux at lines 364 and 375. Finally poloidal_convergence_atlas.py:490 labels any nonempty stored boundary diverted, without reading topology.diverted or checking equality with the saddle flux. The panel faithfully diagnoses the core method's wall-level output and misstates its class; this is not a painter defect. Repair means carrying and enforcing the governed class and level, then regenerating the atlas rather than recaptioning the existing curve.

The full record with verbatim ledger done-whens, commit-by-commit anomaly notes and absolute log paths is /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reviews/nova/s19-codex-20260916/fsa-audit-solver-wall-read/review.md. Machine-readable followups ready for edit_plan append operations are beside it in findings.json; the numeric mechanism receipt is atlas-level-probe.txt.
